| Q6_K (any Q6_K-based mix) | slow: prefill -33% against Q5_K_M |

## 4. Multi-GPU

- **The last device in `device` receives the output head, the drafter's logits and the
  sampling.** Put the card with the most free VRAM after its layers there.
- **`split-mode tensor`** reduces through the internal AllReduce on Windows (no RCCL),
  staged through pinned host memory, so the slowest card's PCIe link bounds it. Over a
  ~3 GB/s link that costs ~200 ms per 512-token ubatch, which cancels the prefill gain. It
  only has a fast path between two devices of the same backend. The Linux build links
  RCCL, which replaces that AllReduce when every device of the split is a ROCm one
  (Server tab, SDK Tuning, Tensor-split AllReduce `internal` switches back): not yet
  measured (section 8).
- **`split-mode tensor` with a card of the other backend** (measured on Linux, llama.cpp
  v0.6.0): the model aborts while loading unless Tensor-split AllReduce is `none`. With
  `none`, on a 27B Q8_0 at ctx 16384 with KV `f16` and a 3:1 ratio, 2.7k tokens, against
  the layer split: prefill 599 against 1290 t/s and decode 17.6 against 17.8 without a
  drafter, prefill 577 against 1232 and decode 44.7 against 45.0 with the embedded MTP
  head.

## 5. Choices that look better than they measure

| option | result |
| --- | --- |
| a low `spec-draft-n-max`, to stay on the FlashAttention vector kernel | refuted: `n-max 1` gives 9.8 t/s at 93.6k, against 13.0 with no drafter |
| Vulkan instead of ROCm | slower on both halves since #28102: with Q8_0, prefill -27% / -25% and decode -18% / -15% (2.6k / 93.6k), per-step cost +20-24% despite a slightly higher draft acceptance. With Q5_K_M the gap widens: prefill -22% / -23%, decode -41% / -30%, per-step cost +50-55%. Its only edge is a compute buffer ~690 MiB smaller. (On v0.4.0, before #28102, Vulkan had won both.) |

## 6. Checking a configuration

- `tools\report-memory.ps1`: requested vs actual VRAM per device, and spill into shared
  memory. A spill is real only above ~512 MiB beyond the pinned `*_Host` buffers.
- `tools\pcie-bench\`: the card's real host link.
- Benchmark tab / `llama-cpp-config bench sweep`: measure at 2.6k and ~90k context, with
  temp 0 and 3 repetitions.

## 7. Linux

Measured with llama.cpp v0.6.0 (v0.7.0 where a row says so) and the TheRock 10.1.0 dist on the `amdgpu` kernel
driver, with the desktop drawn by another adapter (nothing else on the card). llama.cpp
and ROCm are both newer than in the Windows campaign above: a figure that differs from
sections 2 to 5 is not the OS alone. Model: Qwen3.8-27B Q8_0 with its embedded MTP head,
KV `q8_0`, layer split with a CUDA card. Decode figures are 3 reps of 400 tokens at
temp 0.

| subject | result |
| --- | --- |
| VRAM | 32624 MiB total (`mem_info_vram_total` in the card's DRM sysfs directory). The figures below ran at 32442 MiB used, 182 MiB free, with `mem_info_gtt_used` at its idle 24 MiB throughout |
| run-time growth | 85 to 170 MiB over the idle figure at the first prefill (ubatch `1024`), the same at 2.5k and 43.6k tokens |
| `ubatch-size` | `1024` fits at ctx 262144. Model compute buffer on this card: 1176 MiB at `768` and ctx 196608; at `1024`, 1312 (196608), 1504 (229376) and 1696 to 1736 (262144): it grows with the context as well as with the ubatch |
| prefill, card first with most of the layers | 1122 t/s at 2.4k tokens, 1045 at 43.6k (ubatch `1024`, ctx 262144) |
| last-device role (MTP head, output head, sampling) on this card | costs 1288 MiB of output head, 1024 MiB of draft KV (f16) and 648 MiB of draft compute at ctx 262144. Against the same role on a CUDA card: decode 36.3 -> 38.9 t/s at 2.5k tokens (+7%) and 28.5 -> 29.8 at 43.6k (+4.5%), draft acceptance unchanged; prefill at 43.6k 1045 -> 1010 t/s (-3%) |
| `cache-type-k/-v` `f16` against `q8_0` (llama.cpp v0.7.0) | decode at a 43.7k-token prompt 35.2 and 34.5 t/s against 31.5 and 31.5 (+11%), prefill 1161 against 1164 t/s, short prompts within noise. The KV doubles (64 against 34 KiB per token over 16 layers), but the `q8_0` compute buffer grows with the context: on this card 1712 MiB at 262144 in `q8_0`, 456 MiB at 163840 and 620 at 172032 in `f16`. So `f16` fits ~66% of the context `q8_0` does, not half (campaign `27b-kv-f16-20261016`) |
| checking a configuration | `tools\report-memory.ps1` is Windows-only: read `mem_info_vram_used` and `mem_info_gtt_used` after a prefill |

## 8. Not yet measured (placeholders)

| parameter | current value | question | result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT` with the staged runtime | unset | is hipBLASLt (`1`) faster than Tensile (`0`) for BF16/F16 models? | TBD |
| `batch-size` | default (2048) | does a larger logical batch help prefill at the chosen `ubatch-size`? | TBD |
| `cache-ram` | 20480 | restore time of the prompt cache over the card's link | TBD |
| `parallel` > 1 | 1 | aggregate throughput versus the context divided per slot | TBD |
| `spec-draft-p-min` | default | does a confidence floor lift acceptance on the reasoning phase? | TBD |
| embedded MTP vs DFlash2 | DFlash2 | speed and memory of each on this card | TBD |
| power limit | stock (300 W) | prefill and decode per watt | TBD |
| `split-mode tensor` on two R9700 | n/a | real AllReduce cost; `GGML_CUDA_AR_COPY_THRESHOLD` / `_CHUNK_BYTES` (seeds: 1-2 MiB, 2 MiB) | TBD |

===== docs/nvidia_rtx4070_super.md =====
# NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4070 Super: configuration reference

Reference settings for running llama.cpp on the RTX 4070 Super (Ada, `sm_89`, 12 GB)
under Windows, through the CUDA backend. Values were measured on this framework with
llama.cpp v0.5.0. Section 7 holds what was measured again on Linux, with llama.cpp v0.6.0 unless a row says v0.7.0.

## 1. Runtime prerequisites

| item | requirement | why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| cuBLAS | CUDA 13 `cublas64_13.dll` + `cublasLt64_13.dll` next to `llama-server.exe` (pinned: 13.8.0.4) | without them the CUDA backend is skipped silently and the card falls back to Vulkan. The installer keeps the pair across upgrades |
| cudart | linked statically | nothing to install |
| CUDA toolkit (build) | newest installed version, picked by `02-build.ps1` | |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_QUANTS` (build) | q5_0/q5_1 pairs added (`cmake-options.psd1`) | without them a q5 KV cache converts to f16 on every short decode step |

## 2. Device facts

- **The card is enumerated twice**, once by CUDA and once by Vulkan. Pin the CUDA id.
  An unselected device still holds ~160 MiB per llama-server child, because every child
  opens a driver context on each device it enumerates.
- **Usable VRAM is about 11.9 GiB dedicated** (measured at 11.93 GiB with no paging, the
  driver trimming other processes to make room); that leaves nothing for any other
  application on the card. The CUDA driver keeps 290 to 360 MiB of
  non-local (shared) memory in every configuration, even with 1.5 GiB free. That memory
  is not a spill; a spill is real only above ~512 MiB beyond the pinned buffers.

## 3. Settings

### Preset (presets.ini)

| key | value | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `mmproj-device` | off this card, when it is the VRAM constraint | the image encoder (~1.14 GiB with its compute buffer for a BF16 Qwen3.8 mmproj) only computes on image requests. Moving it off and giving this card two more blocks: neutral at 2.6k, +2.4% decode at 93.6k |
| `flash-attn` | omit (`auto` resolves to on) | a quantized KV cache needs FlashAttention |
| `cache-type-k/-v` | `f16` at the context you need; `q8_0` only to fit a longer one | *Linux* (section 7): `f16` decodes 11% faster deep in the context, and its compute buffer is smaller. `q5_1` decodes 6% slower than `q8_0`; `iq4_nl` has no CUDA FlashAttention kernel |
| `override-tensor` | `^token_embd\.weight=<this card>` | moves the table (~1.26 GiB at Q8_0) out of pinned host RAM, where Windows reports it as shared GPU memory: +1-2% decode. The quant must have a CUDA `get_rows` kernel (all K/IQ quants do since b10089) |
| `backend-sampling` | `true` when this card holds the logits | the sampler runs on the GPU instead of copying the logits to the host every token |
| `ubatch-size` | the largest that fits: `768` | each ubatch token costs ~0.53 MiB of model compute buffer, plus a drafter's logits (ubatch x vocab) where they land: `768` adds ~400 MiB over `512` on a last device carrying a DFlash2 drafter. Against `512`: +5.8% prefill at 2.6k, nothing at 93.6k, decode unchanged. `1024` did not fit next to a 27B Q8_0 at ctx 196608 (on Linux it does: section 7) |

### Embedded MTP head (runs entirely on the last device)

With a layer split, the MTP head is the model's last block, so its weights, its draft
KV and its compute all land on the last device. Measured with this card last (Qwen3.8-27B
Q8_0, 3 reps, temp 0):

| key | value | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `spec-draft-n-max` | `4` as a compromise (`3` at long context) | decode at 2.6k: n-max 2/3/4 = 38.0 / 45.9 / **50.6**; at 93.6k: 23.9 / **30.1** / 29.3 (3 and 4 tie within the text difference). `2` costs 21-25% |
| `spec-draft-type-k/-v` | `q8_0` | the draft KV spans the FULL context: ~2 MiB per 1k cells in q8_0, ~4 MiB in f16 (416 against 832 MiB at 212k cells). f16 gives identical text and acceptance and +1% decode at 93.6k only. With v0.6.0 on Linux `q8_0` is the LARGER choice: section 7 |
| memory | draft KV + ~1 GiB of draft compute on this card | at a context of ~200k, move about two blocks of a 27B Q8_0 model off this card to make room |

## 4. Multi-GPU

- **As the last device in `device`, the card receives the output head, a drafter's
  logits and compute, and the sampling.** Budget for them before giving it layers: with a
  27B Q8_0 model, a DFlash2 drafter and ctx 196608 that is ~1 GiB of model compute,
  ~0.5 GiB of drafter compute and the KV of its own layers.
- **An embedded MTP head adds its own draft context on the last device** (a full-context
  KV plus ~1 GiB of compute): budget for it by giving the card fewer layers (see
  section 3).
- **`split-mode tensor`** needs NCCL (Linux only, linked when its dist is installed)
  or two devices of the same backend for its internal AllReduce. Paired with a card of
  another backend (measured on Linux, llama.cpp v0.6.0) the model aborts while loading
  unless Server tab, SDK Tuning, Tensor-split AllReduce is `none`; with `none`, on a 27B
  Q8_0 at ctx 16384 with KV `f16`, 2.7k tokens, against the layer split: prefill 599
  against 1290 t/s and decode 17.6 against 17.8 without a drafter, prefill 577 against
  1232 and decode 44.7 against 45.0 with the embedded MTP head.

## 5. Choices that look better than they measure

| option | result |
| --- | --- |
| this card first in a split | llama-bench prefill improves (up to 1500 t/s) while live decode drops by 22%: llama-bench placement wins do not carry over to live. Not reproduced on Linux (section 7) |
| handing the last-device role (MTP head, output head, sampling) to a HIP card to free this one | with an embedded MTP head, decode -23% at 2.6k and -15% at 93.6k, +21 ms per speculative step. Not the sampler (host sampling is worse) and barely the output head (2.7 ms of the 21): the MTP draft loop, three batch-1 passes with a sync each, is latency-bound and runs faster on this card. On Linux the sign is reversed (section 7) |

## 6. Checking a configuration

- `tools\report-memory.ps1`: requested vs actual VRAM, and spill into shared memory.
- `tools\pcie-bench\`: the card's real host link.
- Benchmark tab / `llama-cpp-config bench sweep`: measure at 2.6k and ~90k context, with
  temp 0 and 3 repetitions.

## 7. Linux

Measured with llama.cpp v0.6.0 (v0.7.0 where a row says so), CUDA 13.4.2 and driver 595.99.02, with the desktop drawn
by another adapter (nothing else on the card). llama.cpp is newer than in the Windows
campaign above, and so is the ROCm dist of the HIP card this one was paired with: a
figure that differs from sections 2 to 5 is not the OS alone. Model: Qwen3.8-27B Q8_0
with its embedded MTP head, KV `q8_0`, layer split with a HIP card. Decode figures are
3 reps of 400 tokens at temp 0.

| subject | result |
| --- | --- |
| usable VRAM | 11876 MiB: `nvidia-smi` counts 406 MiB of `memory.reserved` inside its 12282 MiB total. Read `memory.free`, never total minus used. A process holds ~270 MiB beyond the buffers its load log lists |
| over-allocation | fails, never spills. At load: `cudaMalloc failed: out of memory` and the model does not load. At run time: the CUDA pool takes ~40 MiB more at the first prefill (the same at 2.5k and 43.6k tokens), and a configuration that had loaded with ~20 MiB free aborted the child there (`cuMemCreate`). A clean load proves nothing: run a prefill. The figures below ran with 214 MiB free |
| `ubatch-size` | `1024` fits next to a 27B Q8_0 at ctx 262144. Model compute buffer on this card: 1206 MiB at `768` and ctx 196608; at `1024`, 1352 (196608), 1544 (229376) and 1696 to 1736 (262144): it grows with the context as well as with the ubatch |
| MTP draft context, card last | KV in f16: 768 / 896 / 1024 MiB at 196608 / 229376 / 262144 cells. Compute at ctx 196608: 390 MiB at ubatch `768`, 520 at `1024`; 648 at `1024` and ctx 262144 |
| `spec-draft-type-k/-v` | `f16`. `q8_0` halves the draft KV (544 MiB at 262144 cells) but the draft compute buffer goes from 648 to 1696 MiB: a net +568 MiB |
| last-device role (MTP head, output head, sampling) on a HIP card, this card first | decode 36.3 -> 38.9 t/s at 2.5k tokens (+7%) and 28.5 -> 29.8 at 43.6k (+4.5%), draft acceptance unchanged; prefill at 43.6k 1045 -> 1010 t/s (-3%). The opposite sign of both rows of section 5 |
| `cache-type-k/-v` `f16` against `q8_0` (llama.cpp v0.7.0) | decode at a 43.7k-token prompt 35.2 and 34.5 t/s against 31.5 and 31.5 (+11%), prefill unchanged, short prompts within noise. The KV doubles (64 against 34 KiB per token over 16 layers), but the `q8_0` model compute buffer grows with the context: on this card 1736 MiB at 262144 in `q8_0` (ubatch `1024`), 604 at 163840 and 620 at 172032 in `f16`. So `f16` fits ~66% of the context `q8_0` does, not half (campaign `27b-kv-f16-20261016`) |
| checking a configuration | `tools\report-memory.ps1` is Windows-only: read `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used,memory.free,memory.reserved` after a prefill |

## 8. Not yet measured (placeholders)

| parameter | current value | question | result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vulkan instead of CUDA | CUDA | does the Vulkan backend lose anything on this card? | TBD |
| `backend-sampling` on vs off | on | the measured gain at 2.6k and 93.6k | TBD |
| `GGML_CUDA_FORCE_MMQ` / cuBLAS path | default | prefill at the chosen `ubatch-size` | TBD |
| CUDA graphs (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS`) | default (on) | decode cost or gain with a drafter's variable batch | TBD |
| `token_embd` left in host RAM | on the card | the cost of freeing ~1.26 GiB of VRAM | TBD |
| power limit | stock (220 W) | decode per watt | TBD |
| single-card models that fit in 12 GB | n/a | reference prefill/decode per quant | TBD |
| Linux: `ubatch-size` `1024` against `768` | `1024` | the prefill gain at 2.6k and ~90k | TBD |
| Linux: last-device role at ~90k context | on the HIP card | does the decode gain measured at 2.5k and 43.6k hold? | TBD |


===== docs/qwen3.8_27b.md =====
# Qwen3.8-27B: configuration reference (draft)

Reference settings for Qwen3.8-27B (architecture `qwen35`, dense, with an embedded MTP
head) on this framework. The *Linux* rows were measured with llama.cpp v0.7.0 on the
AMD Radeon AI PRO R9700 (32 GB, ROCm backend) and the RTX 4070 SUPER (12 GB, CUDA
backend) in ONE model, layer split, a 12-core AVX-512 host and 64 GB of RAM (kernel 7.0,
TheRock 10.1.0, CUDA 13.4.2). The file is the Q8_0 with its MTP head, the speculator
`draft-mtp`, `Threads` = `ThreadsBatch` = 12. Rows marked v0.6.0 or *Windows* come from
the earlier campaigns of the two card guides (`amd_ai_pro_r9700.md`,
`nvidia_rtx4070_super.md`), which hold the per-card detail.

Measurements: a 43.7k-token prompt with the prompt cache off (two runs, 128 tokens out:
prefill, then decode right after it, deep in the context), and three different short
prompts after a warm-up (code, prose, math; 400 tokens, temperature 0). The MTP head
changes the greedy path, so read the per-run figures beside the mean.

## 1. Model facts

| item | value | source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| blocks | 64 plus the MTP block (65 in the GGUF) | `block_count = 65`, `nextn_predict_layers = 1`; `n_layer = 64` |
| attention | on every 4th block: 16 layers hold a KV cache; 24 query heads, 4 KV heads, head size 256 | `full_attention_interval = 4`; `llama_kv_cache: 16 layers` |
| Memory row (Model info) | hybrid (attention + recurrent) | `llm_arch_is_hybrid` |
| native context | 262144 | `context_length` |
| GGUF sampling defaults | temp 1.0, top-k 20, top-p 0.95 | `general.sampling.*` |
| file (Q8_0) | 27.04 GiB, 27.32 B parameters | `file size`, `model params` |
| KV cache per token | 64 KiB in `f16`, 34 KiB in `q8_0` (16 layers) | `KV buffer size`: 8704 MiB at 262144 cells in `q8_0`, 10240 at 163840 in `f16` |
| recurrent state at one slot | 598.50 MiB (with the MTP head's sequences) | `llama_memory_recurrent: size` |
| MTP draft context | its own KV over the full context, `f16`: 672 MiB at 172032 cells, 1024 at 262144 | `llama_kv_cache: ... 1 layers` |
| prefill cost by position | v0.6.0, 234k-token prompt: 0.95 ms plus 0.0287 ms per 1k of context per token, 984 t/s at 4k against 142 t/s at 218k | marginal cost per token over one long prompt |
| saved state (`SaveStateOnShutdown`) | ~36 KiB per token held in `q8_0` (4.38 GiB for 127,579 tokens, v0.6.0) | the snapshot file |
| `split-mode tensor` with a card of the other backend | aborts at load unless Tensor-split AllReduce is `none`; with `none` it loses (section 5) | v0.6.0 |

## 2. Recommended settings (two cards)

| key | value | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quant | Q8_0 | on the R9700 (*Windows*) the fastest of the 27B files measured, prefill and decode; Q5_K_M -13%/-10% prefill and -7%/-15% decode, Q6_K mixes slower still |
| `cache-type-k/-v` | `f16`, at the largest context that fits: `172032` | *Linux*: decode at 43.7k tokens 35.2 and 34.5 t/s against 31.5 and 31.5 in `q8_0` at 262144 (+11%), prefill 1161 and 1162 against 1158 and 1169, three prompts 37.7 against 38.9 (36.4/33.4/43.4 against 37.3/34.9/44.4, the same draft acceptance). `q8_0` halves the KV but the compute buffers grow with it: 1712 and 1736 MiB on the two cards at 262144, against 456 and 604 in `f16` at 163840, so `f16` reaches 172032, not 131072 (section 3) (campaign `27b-kv-f16-20261016`) |
| `ctx-size` | `172032` with `f16` | 180224 and 196608 do not load (`cudaMalloc failed: out of memory`); 172032 leaves 281 MB free on the R9700 and 829 MiB on the RTX 4070 SUPER after a 2.9k-token prompt, prefill 1165 and decode 35.2 at 43.7k |
| `device` | the R9700 last | the last device takes the MTP head, the output head and the sampling: *Linux*, v0.6.0, decode +7% at 2.5k tokens and +4.5% at 43.6k against the RTX 4070 SUPER last, prefill -3% (the sign is reversed on *Windows*). Repeated on v0.7.0 with the Coder390 Q8_0 MTP file at 172032 `f16`: at 43.7k decode 31.2 t/s (twice) against 31.3 with the RTX 4070 SUPER last at split 52,14, which leaves it 28 MiB free, and 29.8 at 53,13 (430 MiB free); prefill 1133 and 1123 against 1069 and 1071; short prompt 38.1 against 36.5 and 36.6. With the RTX 4070 SUPER last, 50,16 and 48,18 do not load (its compute buffer finds no room) (campaign `coder390-device-order-20261019`) |
| `tensor-split` | most layers on the R9700, the RTX 4070 SUPER keeping ~800 MiB free | the split that fits 172032 `f16` with `blk\.15\.ffn_(up|down)` pinned to the R9700 |
| `override-tensor` | `token_embd\.weight` on the RTX 4070 SUPER | keeps the embedding table (~1.26 GiB) out of pinned host RAM: +1-2% decode (*Windows*) |
| `split-mode` | `layer` | `tensor` with `none` AllReduce: prefill 599 against 1290 t/s, decode 17.6 against 17.8 without a drafter, 44.7 against 45.0 with MTP (v0.6.0, ctx 16384) |
| `ubatch-size` | `1024` | fits at ctx 262144 in `q8_0` (v0.6.0); against `512`, +5% prefill at 2.6k and 93.6k on the R9700 (*Windows*); decode unaffected |
| `spec-type` | `draft-mtp` | the head is in the GGUF. *Windows*, `spec-draft-n-max 4`: 50.6 t/s at 2.6k tokens; *Linux*, v0.6.0, unassisted (llama-bench `tg2048`): 17.9 |
| `spec-draft-type-k/-v` | `f16` | *Linux*, v0.6.0: `q8_0` halves the draft KV (544 MiB at 262144) but raises the draft compute buffer from 648 to 1696 MiB |
| `load-mode` | `none` | every weight is on a GPU: nothing to map |
| `mmproj-device` | the R9700 | the image encoder (~1.14 GiB with its compute buffer) only computes on image requests; off the smaller card it frees room for two blocks (*Windows*: +2.4% decode at 93.6k) |

## 3. KV cache type and context (*Linux*, v0.7.0)

Both cards, the R9700 last, `ubatch-size 1024`, `draft-mtp` with an `f16` draft KV (campaign
`27b-kv-f16-20261016`):

| KV, context | free after a 2.9k prompt (R9700 / RTX 4070 SUPER) | prefill t/s at 43.7k | decode at 43.7k | decode, three prompts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `q8_0`, 262144 | 196 MB / 223 MiB | 1158, 1169 | 31.5, 31.5 | 38.9 |
| `f16`, 163840 | 878 MB / 989 MiB | 1161, 1162 | 35.2, 34.5 | 37.7 |
| **`f16`, 172032** | 281 MB / 829 MiB | 1165 | **35.2** | n/a |
| `f16`, 180224 and 196608 | do not load | | | |

The gap grows with the tokens held, as an attention that reads a quantized KV would,
and does not show on short prompts. The same holds on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next
(`qwen3.8_flash_next.md`, section 9). For conversations that grow turn by turn, decode
deep in the context is what every turn pays; `q8_0` stays the choice only where a context
above 172032 is needed.

## 4. Linux against Windows (v0.7.0)

The same machine and preset on both systems: the Coder390 Q8_0 MTP file, 172032 `f16`,
the R9700 last, `ubatch-size 1024`, the same llama.cpp build and prompts, three
repetitions of 256 tokens at temperature 0 (campaigns `coder390-device-order-20261019`
for *Linux*, `coder390-windows-vs-linux-20261020` for *Windows*):

| prompt | prefill t/s *Linux* / *Windows* | decode t/s *Linux* / *Windows* | draft acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 43.7k tokens | 1133, 1123 / 1030 (-9%) | 31.2, 31.2 / 25.3 (-19%) | 53% / 51% |
| 100 tokens | 225, 226 / 209 (-7%) | 38.1, 38.1 / 31.0 (-19%) | 61% / 59% |

Weights and KV per card are the same to the MiB and the draft acceptance is within two
points, so the gap is the time of each step (39.6 against ~32 ms per token at 43.7k), not
the placement or the drafter. `Threads` was 18 on *Windows* against 12, inert with every
layer on a GPU. *Windows* lists the CPU's integrated GPU as a ROCm device too, ahead of
the R9700, so a preset copied from *Linux* names the wrong card until its device ids are
renumbered.

## 5. Choices that look better than they measure

| option | result |
| --- | --- |
| `q8_0` KV to fit the native context | fits 262144, but costs 11% of decode at 43.7k tokens (section 3) |
| `split-mode tensor` across the two backends | half the prefill of the layer split, no decode gain (section 2) |
| Vulkan on the R9700 instead of ROCm | *Windows*, Q8_0: prefill -27% / -25% and decode -18% / -15% at 2.6k / 93.6k |
| a low `spec-draft-n-max`, to stay on the FlashAttention vector kernel | *Windows*: `n-max 1` gives 9.8 t/s at 93.6k, against 13.0 with no drafter; `2` costs 21-25% against `4` |
| `q8_0` draft KV | larger, not smaller, on v0.6.0 and later (section 2) |

## 6. Not yet measured (placeholders)

| parameter | current value | question | result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec-draft-n-max` on *Linux*, v0.7.0 | default | does `4` (`3` at long context) still win with the R9700 last? | TBD |
| decode at 87k+ tokens in `f16` | n/a | does the gap over `q8_0` keep growing with the context? | TBD |
| the IQ3_S file with its MTP head | `q8_0`, 393216, `parallel 2` | the same `f16` question at a context it can afford | TBD |
| `reasoning-preserve`, `ctx-checkpoints` | default | the hybrid model reprocesses from a checkpoint on a divergent prompt: the cost on this model's 598.5 MiB recurrent state | TBD |


===== docs/qwen3.8_flash_next.md =====
# Qwen3.8-Flash-Next: configuration reference (draft)

Reference settings for Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO (architecture `qwen4exp`) on this
framework. Measured with llama.cpp v0.7.0 on Windows, on the AMD Radeon AI PRO R9700
(32 GB, ROCm backend, `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0`) and, for the MoE cache, the RTX 4070
SUPER (12 GB, CUDA backend), with a 12-core AVX-512 host and 64 GB of RAM.
Decode figures are one run of 200 tokens after a 25-token prompt; the prompt figures are
too short to stand for prefill (section 2, prefill row).

**Linux rows** (marked *Linux*) come from the same host booted into Linux (kernel 7.0,
TheRock 10.1.0, CUDA 13.4.2) with BOTH cards in one model, layer split, the IQ3_S at ctx
131072, `q8_0` KV cache, ubatch 512, `op-offload false`, the experts of 14 layers in RAM
(`n-cpu-moe 14`) and `spec-type ngram-map-k`: three runs per value of 200 tokens after a
25-token prompt (72 with the chat template) unless stated, `Threads` = `ThreadsBatch` = 22 up
to the `threads` row and 12 from it on. llama.cpp v0.7.0 unless marked v0.6.0. This is the
layout the campaign started from; the recommended one (`f16`, `n-cpu-moe 16`) is in section 9.

## 1. Model facts

| item | value | source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| layers | 48; attention on every 4th (12 layers hold a KV cache), recurrent state on all 48 | GGUF header (`full_attention_interval = 4`); `llama_kv_cache: 12 layers` |
| Memory row (Model info) | hybrid (attention + recurrent) | `llm_arch_is_hybrid` |
| experts | 512 per layer, 10 routed per token, plus a shared expert | `expert_count`, `expert_used_count` |
| native context | 262144 | `context_length` |
| GGUF sampling defaults | temp 1.0, top-k 20, top-p 0.95 | `general.sampling.*` |
| chat template | reads `enable_thinking`, `preserve_thinking`, `reasoning_effort` | template source: `reasoning-preserve` and `reasoning-effort` are live levers |
| files (IQ3_S) | shard 1: 51.05 GiB of weights; shard 2: 26.82 GiB, the n-gram table `per_layer_token_embd` (27465 MiB) | file sizes; `add: tensor per_layer_token_embd.weight ... lazy read enabled` |
| non-expert weights on the GPU | 3983 MiB (every expert in RAM, `n-cpu-moe 48`) | `model buffer size` |
| fixed state at one slot | recurrent 112.57 MiB; KV 24 + 96 MiB per 4096 tokens of context (f16) | `RS buffer size`, `KV buffer size` at ctx 4096 and 32768 |
| `split-mode tensor` | not supported for `qwen4exp` (#28569) | `gguf::arch_supports_sm_tensor` |

## 2. Recommended settings

| key | value | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `lazy-mode` | `auto` (the default) | the 26.8 GiB n-gram table is above the 4 GiB threshold and stays mapped; no run read it into memory. *Linux*: `on`, `auto` and `off` decode 25.1 ±1.2, 25.4 ±0.4 and 25.9 ±0.4 t/s, noise; `on` and `auto` both print "lazy read enabled" and map the table (`CPU_Mapped`), `off` reads it into 27466 MiB of RAM for no gain |
| `n-cpu-moe` | the lowest that leaves ~500 MB free on each card (below that, hipBLASLt's workspace runs out on the first real prompt and the process hangs in the driver; a 5-token test does not show it). On a 32 GB card at ctx 32768, `26` (27170 MiB of weights on the card, 1017 MiB compute buffer, ~3 GiB left) | decode 17.8 t/s at `26` against 12.3 at `48` (both `mmap`). *Linux*, ubatch 512, the lowest with >= 512 MB free on the R9700 after a 2.9k-token prompt, decode on the 25-token prompt (two runs): see the table below |
| `moe-cache-mib` | `0` (off), with no condition, on llama.cpp v0.7.0 (`620d2225c`; the cache dates from #29887, 2026-10-07): a cache policy upstream changes would reopen it | R9700: a loss of 3-4x (14.1 to 3.0-4.0 t/s). RTX 4070 SUPER: at equal VRAM, experts kept on the card beat the cache at every split (25.6 t/s against 19.5-23.6), and with every expert in RAM it is noise (section 3). *Linux*, two cards, the cache on the R9700 in place of resident layers: 17.8 to 22.5 t/s against 27.4 (section 3) |
| `load-mode` | `none` whenever experts sit in RAM. On Windows/ROCm with experts on the GPU, `mmap`, set explicitly, until the abort of section 4 is fixed; on Linux `none` there too (no abort). `auto` resolves to `none` only when a device THE MODEL RUNS ON reports no mmap support (`llama-model.cpp`, `load_tensors`): a Ryzen iGPU visible to HIP counts only when `device` is left blank; with `device` pinned to the discrete cards `auto` is `mmap` | `n-cpu-moe 48`, decode with `none` against `mmap`: R9700 11.6 to 14.1 against 12.3; RTX 4070 SUPER 22.7 to 22.9 against 14.0 to 17.1. `none` loads slower (25 to 43 s against 10 to 11 s). `n-cpu-moe 26` with `none` aborts on the R9700 on Windows. *Linux*: `none` 25.6 ±0.1 t/s; `mmap` 23.9 ±2.6 (21.0 on the first run after the load, 24.9 and 25.9 after); `auto` resolved to `mmap` (log), 24.7 ±1.7. (That AMD advises against `mmap` on ROCm is a reported recommendation with no primary source linked here.) |
| `repack` | leave unset | it acts only with `no-host` (next row): without it the GPU's pinned host buffer type is picked before `CPU_REPACK`, so nothing is repacked: the MoE cache layout is identical with and without `--no-repack` (section 3). *Linux*, `n-cpu-moe 14`, `moe-cache-mib 512`: the same 4 groups and 508.50 MiB with and without `--no-repack`, no `CPU_REPACK` buffer in either log |
| `no-host` | `true` with `op-offload false` | weights kept in RAM skip the GPU's pinned host buffer (`ROCm_Host`), so the CPU repacks the types it has interleaved kernels for: here `ffn_down_exps` in IQ4_NL (4950 MiB of the 14997 MiB in RAM at `n-cpu-moe 16`; `gate`/`up` in IQ2_S/IQ3 stay as they are, 10047 MiB of ordinary RAM). The pinned buffer only speeds the per-batch copies of `op-offload true`, which `false` never makes. *Linux*, the recommended preset (section 9), 43.7k-token prompt: prefill 416 and 419 t/s against 383 and 382, decode 25.0 against 24.9, three prompts 27.3 both. With `op-offload true` (section 8) the copies would come from pageable memory: not measured (campaign `flash-next-no-host-20261016`) |
| `spec-type` | `ngram-map-k`, `spec-ngram-map-k-size-m 64` (`size-n` left at 12) | no MTP head in the GGUF, so the n-gram drafters are the only speculators. *Linux*, v0.6.0, IQ3_XXS (section 7): code rewrite 61-65 t/s (`size-m` 48 and 64) against 28.6 with none; prose reasoning 40.0 ±1.6 against 29.4 ±1.3 |
| prefill | (measured) | *Linux*, ubatch 512: 368 and 372 t/s at 2.9k tokens, 364 and 370 at 43.7k, 340 and 341 at 87.4k; decode right after: 24.5, 20.5 and 17.5 t/s. v0.6.0 gave 342 and 338 at 43.7k |
| `threads` | one per physical core (12 on the 12-core host), `ThreadsBatch` the same | *Linux*, 43.7k-token prompt, two runs of 128 tokens: prefill 410 and 405 t/s at 12, 363 and 368 at 16, 371 and 377 at 22, 364 and 368 at 24; decode 21.8, 21.5, 21.1 and 17.6 |
| `ubatch-size` | 512 at the lowest `n-cpu-moe` that fits, with `op-offload false`; for long prompts, 8192 with `op-offload true` (section 8) | with `op-offload false` a larger ubatch is paid in experts moved to RAM (section 5). *Linux*: the R9700's compute buffer is 877 MiB at 512, 1496 at 1024 and 2125 at 2048, so at `n-cpu-moe 14` neither 1024 nor 2048 loads |
| `cache-type-k/-v` | `f16` | `q8_0` halves the KV buffers but raises the compute buffers, so it saves only one layer of experts at 131072 and two at 262144 (table below), and it costs decode as the context fills: *Linux*, 43.7k-token prompt, 21.8 t/s in `q8_0` at `n-cpu-moe 14` against 24.9 in `f16` at `16`, 20.8 against 23.4 at 262144 (section 9) |
| `reasoning-preserve` | leave unset (on since v0.4.0) | *Linux*, turn 2 of a chat that sends turn 1's thinking back: on, the history is replayed unchanged and only the new 19 tokens are processed (prompt 204); `false` strips the old thinking, so the prompt is shorter (118) but diverges inside turn 1 and 60 tokens are reprocessed from a checkpoint. `false` is worth it only when long thinking fills the context |
| `ctx-checkpoints` | leave unset (32, at least 8192 tokens apart) | the recurrent state cannot be rolled back, so a prompt that diverges from the cached one resumes from the last checkpoint before the divergence or from zero: *Linux*, the `reasoning-preserve false` turn above reprocesses 60 of 118 tokens with checkpoints and all 118 with `0`. A checkpoint is 112.6 MiB of host memory, up to 3.5 GiB at 32 |

*Linux* `n-cpu-moe` per context and KV type (`override-tensor = output\.weight=ROCm0` only;
VRAM free on the R9700 at that value; decode t/s):

| context, KV | both cards | R9700 alone (`device = ROCm0`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 131072, `q8_0` | `15`, split 40,9: 843 MB, 25.7 ±2.3 | `25`: 861 MB, 21.3 ±2.6 |
| 131072, `f16` | `16`, split 40,9: 645 MB, 26.9 ±0.2 | `26`: 545 MB, 21.8 ±1.5 |
| 262144, `q8_0` | `18`, split 41,8: 368 MB, 25.8 ±0.1 | `28`: 1318 MB, 21.1 ±1.5 |
| 262144, `f16` | `21`, split 41,8: 839 MB, 23.3 ±2.8 | `30`: 650 MB, 21.3 ±0.1 |

At 262144 the RTX 4070 SUPER's compute buffer
(1601 MiB with `q8_0`) no longer fits at split 40,9 however many experts go to RAM, since
`n-cpu-moe` frees the first layers, which sit on the R9700: the split must give the small
card a layer less. The `q8_0` KV cache halves the KV buffers but raises the compute
buffers (877 against 533 MiB on the R9700 at 131072), so it saves one layer at 131072 and
two at 262144. The second card is worth ten layers of experts and 20-25% of decode. The
`n-cpu-moe 14` of the *Linux* rows at 131072 `q8_0` comes from also moving
`blk\.38\.ffn_down_exps` to the RTX 4070 SUPER.

## 3. MoE cache and repack (what the init prints)

- **The cache groups layers by expert tensor types.** At `n-cpu-moe 48` the IQ3_S file
  gives seven groups (IQ4_NL `ffn_down_exps` on some layers, Q2_0 on the others), each at
  ~4.2% of its experts as slots in 2048 MiB, for 47962.50 MiB of host experts.
- **Repack happens only with `--no-host`** (the preset's `no-host`). With it,
  `CPU_REPACK` takes 17550 MiB of experts, those layers leave the cache, and 9 layers stay
  cacheable (7112.50 MiB of host experts, ~28.7% as slots in 2048 MiB). Without it, the
  default and `--no-repack` print the same 48-layer cache.
- **Each miss uploads the expert over the host link**: at 50% hits that is ~470 MiB per
  token, so the result follows the card's real link more than its compute.
- **On two cards the cache sits with the layers it serves.** *Linux*, `n-cpu-moe 14`,
  `moe-cache-mib 512`: the 14 host-expert layers belong to the R9700's share of the layer
  split, and the whole cache (508.50 MiB for 12650 MiB of host experts, 4 groups at
  3.7-4.0% slots) went to the R9700, none to the RTX 4070 SUPER. Size the cache against
  the free VRAM of the card holding the first layers, not by the split proportions.

Decode at `n-cpu-moe 48`, ctx 32768, `load-mode none`, 200 tokens (t/s; hit rate at
ubatch <= 8):

| `moe-cache-mib` | R9700 | RTX 4070 SUPER |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 14.1, 11.6 | 22.7, 22.9 |
| 2048 (~4.2% slots) | 3.0 (50%) | 23.2 (51%) |
| 4096 (~8.5% slots) | 4.0 (64%) | 23.9, 19.7 (62-64%) |
| 6144 (~12.8% slots) | n/a | 25.0 (71%), but 1.2 GiB over the free VRAM: the fit cannot correct it with `-ngl` set, so the driver spills to shared memory; not a usable setting |

With `mmap` the cache is a loss on both cards. RTX 4070 SUPER: 17.1 and
14.0 t/s without the cache, 13.4 (50%) at 2048 MiB, 10.4 (62%) at 4096. R9700: 12.3
without, 2.8 at 2048 MiB.

**The same VRAM as layers or as cache** (RTX 4070 SUPER, `load-mode none`, ctx 32768,
two runs each; a layer of experts is ~1.1 GiB on the card):

| configuration | projected VRAM | decode t/s | hit rate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `n-cpu-moe 45`, no cache | 9572 MiB | 25.8, 25.4 | n/a |
| `n-cpu-moe 46`, 1024 MiB | 9453 MiB | 19.7, 19.4 | 39% |
| `n-cpu-moe 47`, 2048 MiB | 9416 MiB | 21.8, 21.6 | 50% |
| `n-cpu-moe 48`, 3072 MiB | 9136 MiB | 23.8, 23.4 | 59% |

*Linux*, both cards, the preset of the *Linux* rows with 12 threads, the cache on the R9700
(three runs each):

| configuration | cache | decode t/s | hit rate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `n-cpu-moe 14`, no cache | n/a | 27.4 ±0.2 | n/a |
| `n-cpu-moe 15`, `moe-cache-mib 896` | 892.97 MiB for 13712.50 MiB of host experts | 17.8 ±0.0 | 45% |
| `n-cpu-moe 16`, `moe-cache-mib 1792` | 1789.53 MiB for 14675.00 MiB | 19.9 ±1.4 | 65% |
| `n-cpu-moe 17`, `moe-cache-mib 2688` | 2683.30 MiB for 15737.50 MiB | 22.5 ±0.1 | 74% |

The smallest cache is the worst, so no "one hot layer in cache" sweet spot exists here.

*Linux*, the same with `no-host` (section 2): the CPU repacks the IQ4_NL `ffn_down_exps`, so
only the five host layers with Q2_0 ones (1, 8, 10, 12, 13; 3787.50 MiB) stay cacheable,
and the cache spends far fewer synchronizations per token:

| configuration | decode t/s, three prompts | hit rate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `n-cpu-moe 16`, no cache | 27.0 (26.7, 27.2, 27.2) | n/a |
| `n-cpu-moe 17`, `moe-cache-mib 1024` | 26.0 (25.9, 26.2, 25.8) | 80% |
| `n-cpu-moe 18`, `moe-cache-mib 2048` | 26.6 (26.8, 26.4, 26.5) | 93% |

Close to even, no longer a loss of 18-35%, but still no gain: at 93% hits the cache still trails
the two resident layers it replaced. A large cache does no better: without `no-host`, `n-cpu-moe 28` and
`moe-cache-mib 12000` (11993.90 MiB for 26875.00 MiB of host experts, every host layer
cacheable) decode 25.8 t/s on the three prompts (25.7, 26.6, 25.0) at 92% hits (campaign `flash-next-moe-cache-20261016`).
The cache's log has no per-layer statistics (`log_stats` prints two totals); the
aggregate is enough for the verdict, and the per-layer spread would only steer a manual
`override-tensor`, which is worth it only if an upstream fix still leaves the cache losing.

**The hit rate is high; serving the misses is what costs.** 4.2% of the experts as slots
gives 50% hits, 8.5% gives 62-64%: the routing is skewed. Each miss is served per layer
(`llama-moe-cache.cpp`, v0.7.0): the router's ids are read back with a
`ggml_backend_synchronize` (48 stalls per token), then one `ggml_backend_tensor_set_async`
per run of consecutive experts and per tensor, ~15 small transfers per layer and ~720 per
token at 0.3-1 MB each. On the R9700 the cache moves ~1.3 GB/s, far below any PCIe
link, so bandwidth is not the limit. (330 - 70) ms per token over ~720 transfers gives
~0.36 ms each, a fixed cost per transfer on the HIP path on Windows (WDDM submission);
on the RTX 4070 SUPER the same count fits within 44 ms per token. **That 0.36 ms is a
derived estimate, not a measurement**: the difference of two mean token times over an
estimated count of transfers, with no trace behind it (section 6).

Two changes that would target the cost, for an upstream issue:

- **Defer the router read by one layer.** Read layer l's ids while layer l+1 computes
  and start its uploads a layer late: one layer of latency on the first token, no
  synchronous stall per layer.
- **Stage the misses contiguously.** Copy a layer's missing experts host-side into one
  pinned staging area, then one `tensor_set_async` per tensor: 3 transfers per layer
  instead of ~15, which matters where the cost is per call.

## 4. Known failures

| configuration | result |
| --- | --- |
| `load-mode none` with experts on the GPU (`n-cpu-moe 26`) | aborts right after the weight upload: `ROCm error: unspecified launch failure` in `ggml_cuda_set_device`, at ctx 4096 and 32768, with and without the MoE cache (4 of 4 runs). It fails at load, not at decode, which points to a host buffer `none` leaves unpinned or an allocation it does not expect; worth a minimal upstream reproduction. Windows only: on *Linux* the same mode with the experts of 34 layers on the GPUs (R9700 and RTX 4070 SUPER in one model) loads and runs in every run since 2026-10-14, v0.6.0 and v0.7.0 |
| `mmap` with experts in RAM | llama.cpp warns "tensor overrides to CPU are used with mmap enabled - consider using --load-mode none"; at `n-cpu-moe 48` decode drops by up to 12.5% on the R9700 and by 25 to 39% on the RTX 4070 SUPER |

## 5. Choices that look better than they measure

| option | result |
| --- | --- |
| the MoE cache, to keep hot experts in VRAM | refuted: 3 to 4.6x slower decode on the R9700; on the RTX 4070 SUPER the same VRAM as resident layers is 8-31% faster, on Linux with two cards 22-54% faster, and with every expert in RAM the cache is noise (`none`) or a loss (`mmap`) (section 3) |
| `--no-repack`, to let repacked layers into the MoE cache | inert: nothing is repacked unless `--no-host` is set |
| a larger `ubatch-size`, paid for with experts in RAM, with `op-offload false` | refuted for prefill (with `op-offload true` it wins, section 8). *Linux*, `n-cpu-moe 26`, ctx 262144, `f16`, 43.7k-token prompt: 248 and 244 t/s at 512, 281 and 282 at 1024, 305 and 308 at 2048; with the preset's ctx 131072 and `q8_0` at 2048, 319 and 313; `n-cpu-moe 14` at 512 gives 364 and 370. `n-cpu-moe 26` holds more experts in RAM than 2048 needs at ctx 131072 (512 needs 15 there, section 2), so the true loss is smaller than this |
| Vulkan (RADV) on the R9700 instead of ROCm, for the IQ expert kernels | refuted. *Linux*, the recommended preset with the R9700 as `Vulkan2` (the RTX 4070 SUPER stays on CUDA): prefill 330 and 336 t/s at 43.7k tokens against 416 and 419, decode right after 7.6 and 9.6 against 25.0, 5.1 on a short prompt |

## 6. Not yet measured (placeholders)

| parameter | current value | question | result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIP upload cost per miss | n/a | a profile of the miss path (sync per layer, transfers per token) on ROCm against CUDA | TBD: needs a trace (rocprofv3, nsys), not a bench run |
| `load-mode none` abort | n/a | minimal reproduction for upstream; Windows only (not on Linux ROCm, section 4), but also CUDA/Vulkan on Windows? | TBD |

## 7. Two cards on Linux: quant and n-gram drafters (v0.6.0)

Both cards in one model as in the *Linux* rows above, each quant with the lowest
`n-cpu-moe` that fits (IQ3_S 14, IQ3_XXS 7; the drafter sweeps below ran at IQ3_XXS `n-cpu-moe` 7 and 8), the same other keys. The drafters change the
greedy path (the verify batches differ numerically), so two runs of one setting can
produce different texts: read the per-run figures, and compare settings on runs that
produced the same kind of output.

| | IQ3_XXS | IQ3_S |
| --- | --- | --- |
| prefill at 43.7k tokens | 478, 485 | 342, 338 |
| decode at 43.7k tokens | 24.0 ±0.2 | 20.9 ±0.1 |
| code rewrite (3.8k-token prompt, ngram-map-k) | 49.9, 56.6, 64.1 | 47.0 ±2.0 |
| prose reasoning (ngram-map-k) | 40.0 ±1.6 (at `n-cpu-moe 8`) | 31.8 ±1.0 |
| quality, model card (BF16 = 93.12) | 92.57 | 93.26 |

The prose gap is partly the drafter's: the IQ3_S text gave it 576
drafts against 1058.

N-gram drafters on the code-rewrite prompt (IQ3_XXS, 8000 tokens max, two runs; decode t/s):

| setting | decode | note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| none | 28.6 | |
| `ngram-mod` | 52.9 | the second run aborted: `CUDA error: out of memory` in `cudaGraphInstantiate` with ~400 MiB free on the 12 GB card |
| `ngram-simple` | 52.3 ±1.3 | |
| `ngram-map-k` | 61.0 ±2.7 | answer 124 t/s at 87-92% accepted, reasoning 35 t/s |
| `ngram-map-k`, `size-n` 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 | 35.0 / 51.5 / 61.3 / 56.0 | 12, the default, is best |
| `ngram-map-k`, `size-m` 16 / 32 / 48 / 64 | 48.2 / 49.7 / 59.8 / 63.6 | the answer (copied text) rises with the draft length: 97, 113, 132, 156 t/s |
| `min-hits` | inert | read only by `ngram-map-k4v` (`common/ngram-map.cpp`); `ngram-simple` and `ngram-map-k` ignore it |

## 8. Long prompts: experts in RAM, uploaded per ubatch (Strata-style)

The layout of Strata (https://github.com/niko1221/strata, which builds on ggml): experts in
RAM, the hot ones in VRAM, prompts read in ubatches of up to 8192 tokens.

*Linux*, both cards, 12 threads, ctx 131072, `q8_0`, `load-mode none`, `op-offload true`,
`cache-ram 2048` (the pinned experts leave little RAM), placement by `override-tensor`
(`blk\.(...)\.ffn_.*_exps=CPU`) rather than `n-cpu-moe`. With `op-offload true` each ubatch
uploads the host experts and the GPUs compute them, so a large ubatch amortizes the upload;
the compute buffer grows with it (4.8 GiB per card at 4096, 9.5 GiB at 8192). Prefill on the
43.7k-token prompt, decode right after it, and decode on three different prompts after a
warm-up (code, prose, math; 400 tokens each, temperature 0):

| layout | prefill t/s | decode at 43.7k | decode, three prompts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| the *Linux* rows: `n-cpu-moe 14`, ubatch 512, `op-offload false` | 410, 405 | 21.8 | 27.4 |
| experts of layers 25-38 on the R9700, 34 in RAM, ubatch 8192 | 669, 674 | 17.4, 17.5 | 21.6 |
| layers 19-38 on the R9700, 28 in RAM, ubatch 4096 | 578, 581 | 18.4, 18.5 | 22.5 |
| layers 25-38 on the R9700, ubatch 4096, the same VRAM as `moe-cache-mib 8704` (75% hits) | 524, 586 | 15.5, 17.3 | 21.1 |
| every expert in RAM, `moe-cache-mib 28672`, ubatch 512, `op-offload false` | n/a | n/a | 24.8 |

A large ubatch buys 40-65% of prefill for 15-20% of decode: worth it where long prompts
dominate, not for chat. The VRAM a smaller ubatch frees is worth more as resident layers
than as MoE cache. The cache cannot reproduce Strata's decode: a miss is uploaded to the
GPU (section 3), where Strata computes the missing experts on the CPU alongside the GPU.
Every expert in RAM pins ~47 GiB (48284 MiB `ROCm_Host`) and, with a 2964 MiB host compute buffer at ubatch 8192,
ran a 59 GiB host into swap: keep part of the experts in VRAM.

## 9. Two cards on Linux: the preset (v0.7.0)

Both cards, 12 threads, ubatch 512, `op-offload false`, `ngram-map-k`; the 43.7k-token
prompt (two runs of 128 tokens) and three different prompts after a warm-up (code, prose,
math; 400 tokens, temperature 0):

| context, KV | `n-cpu-moe`, split | prefill t/s | decode at 43.7k | decode, three prompts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 131072, `q8_0` | 14 (+ `blk\.38\.ffn_down_exps=CUDA0`), 40,9 | 397, 414 | 21.8 | 27.1 |
| **131072, `f16`** | **16, 40,9** | 381, 382 | **24.7, 25.1** | **27.4** |
| 262144, `q8_0` | 18, 41,8 | 349, 351 | 20.9, 20.7 | 26.0 |
| 262144, `f16` | 21, 41,8 | 311, 319 | 23.3, 23.4 | 25.2 |

The recommended preset is the
second row plus `no-host = true` (section 2: prefill 416 and 419 instead of 381 and 382, the
rest unchanged). Against the first row: 3% more prefill, the same decode on short prompts,
and 14% more deep in the context.
After a 2.9k-token prompt it leaves ~500 MB free on the R9700 (the drafter included); one
layer more in RAM if hipBLASLt runs out. 262144 costs 14-17% of prefill and 4-8% of
short-prompt decode against 131072 with the same KV type.

**Decode is mostly a fixed cost per token.** At 131072 `q8_0`, decode on the three prompts
with the experts of the first 15 layers on the CPU, of 16 layers with IQ2_S `gate`/`up`, of
14 layers with IQ3_XXS/IQ3_S `gate`/`up` (13.7 to 14.7 GiB each) and of the first 24 layers:
27.3, 27.3, 26.9 and 23.9 t/s. A layer of experts moved to the CPU costs ~1.3% of decode,
whichever quant type it holds, so one or two layers more or less do not show. During decode
the R9700 is ~75% busy and the RTX 4070 SUPER ~5% (`amd-smi`, `nvidia-smi`); the CPU
threads spin, so their load says nothing. Without the drafter, the preset decodes 27.5 t/s
on prose and math and `n-cpu-moe 24` 23.9 on all three, the same as with it: these
400-token reasoning openings give the n-gram drafter nothing to copy (its gain is on
repeated text, section 7).


===== installer/AGENTS.md =====
# installer\

The NSIS template and the sources of the external distributions. `03-package.ps1` (see [docs\build-pipeline.md](../docs/build-pipeline.md)) stages the binaries and the GPU runtimes and renders the template. The installer is **self-contained for the GPU backends**: nothing is downloaded on the end user's machine, and there is no post-install helper.

- `dist-sources.psd1` says **where** each external distribution is looked up, and holds **no versions**: nothing is pinned, every reader resolves the newest STABLE release at run time (ROCm/TheRock `Dir` + `Marker` + tarball `Indexes`; CUDA `Dir` + `Marker`, redist index, `Platform` and the redist `Components` the toolkit is assembled from; Vulkan SDK `Dir` + `Marker`, latest-version URL, installer template and `Signer`). It also holds the **build machine's dist layout**: `DistRoot` (default `E:\`, overridden by `-DistRoot` on `00`/`01`, `LLAMA_FW_DIST_ROOT`, or the root recorded in `config-build.psd1`) and `DistOs` (`windows`), giving `<DistRoot>\<Dir>\<Version>\<DistOs>`; the helpers that read it are in the root `dists.ps1`. **End users never see that layout**: what the backends load is copied out of it into the installer. It is the **single source** for `00-install-prerequisites.ps1`, `01-configure.ps1` and `common.ps1`. **ROCm host by version**: 10.0+ stables live under `stable.repo.amd.com/rocm/core/tarball/` (`rc.`/`nightly.repo.amd.com` for the others); everything up to 7.14 and the 10.0.0 rcs stay on `repo.amd.com/rocm/tarball-multi-arch/`. A wrong host fails with a `403` that reads exactly like "not published yet", so the URL is FOUND in an index, never composed: HTML with an inline JS `files` array, matched strictly (`\d+\.\d+\.\d+` immediately before the extension) so `-tests-` builds and dated nightlies (`10.1.0a20260831`) are not taken as releases. Prerelease indexes stay out. A new host for a future major means adding its index here.
- **The GPU runtimes are in the package** (`03-package.ps1`, "Bundle the GPU runtimes"; the Windows counterpart of the same section in `03-package.sh`). The target machine needs the GPU drivers, which also bring `vulkan-1.dll`, and the VC++ runtime; no ROCm dist, no `HIP_PATH`, no cuBLAS download.
  - **Which libraries is not a list**: it is the import closure of the staged `.exe`/`.dll` (`Get-PeImports` reads the PE import and delay-import directories, headers only), restricted to what resolves inside a dist: the ROCm dist's `bin\`, the CUDA dist's `bin\x64`, the Vulkan SDK's `Bin\`. Whatever else an import names is a **system library and stays out**: Windows' own DLLs, the VC++ runtime (`MSVCP140`, `VCRUNTIME140*`, `libomp140.x86_64`), the driver's `vulkan-1.dll` (the Vulkan SDK carries the loader only as `Helpers\VulkanRT.exe`, so nothing resolves there today). Today that yields `hipblas`, `rocblas`, `rocsolver`, `libhipblaslt`, `origami`, `amdhip64_7`, `rocm_kpack` and `cublas64_13` + `cublasLt64_13`. The script refuses to package a `ggml-hip.dll` without `hipblas.dll`, or a `ggml-cuda.dll` without `cublas64_*.dll`.
  - **Everything lands flat in `bin\`**, next to `llama-server.exe`, which is also what the dist's own `bin\` looks like. That is a load-order fix as much as a convenience, and it is what makes BF16/F16 run on RDNA4: Windows resolves imports from the exe's dir and then System32, **both before PATH**, and the driver keeps its own `amdhip64_7.dll` in System32, so a runtime reached through PATH means the dist's `rocblas.dll` + `libhipblaslt.dll` running against the DRIVER's HIP runtime, the mix that aborts at the first prefill batch. `rocm_kpack.dll` is `amdhip64_7`'s direct import, so it must come from the same dist.
  - **What no import table shows** is added by name, ROCm only: `amd_comgr*.dll` (asked for by bare name at run time, and System32 holds the driver's build; with the Ryzen iGPU (gfx1036) enabled that mix makes `hipGetDeviceCount` fail with `no ROCm-capable device is detected` and the HIP backend disappears), and the **kernel stores**: `bin\rocblas\library` (Tensile, found relative to `rocblas.dll`; without it the first matmul under `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` dies with `Could not initialize Tensile host`), `bin\hipblaslt\library`, and `.kpack\blas_lib_*.kpack`, which sits beside `bin\` as it does in the dist. Only the architectures in `GpuTargets` are copied (files or per-arch directories alike, plus whatever names no architecture): the CDNA `gfx908`/`gfx90a` half could never be used by a `ggml-hip.dll` with no code for it. About 375 + 465 + 95 MiB instead of 700 + 519 + 1535.
  - The bundle is the runtime of the dist the installer was **built** against. It does not follow a later `HIP_PATH` move on the build machine and does not have to: it changes with the next installer.
- `llama-cpp-framework.nsi.template`: NSIS installer template with placeholders (`@VERSION@` = framework version, `@LLAMA_BUILD@` = the bundled llama.cpp release tag, `@STAGING_DIR@`, `@OUTPUT_FILE@`) replaced by `03-package.ps1` into `build\windows\llama-cpp.nsi` (transient). ARP DisplayName/title: "llama.cpp-framework v@VERSION@ (llama @LLAMA_BUILD@)"; DisplayVersion = framework version; the llama release also goes to `HKLM\Software\llama.cpp\LlamaBuild` (the `Build` name is kept: renaming the registry value would strand existing installs).
  - **`SetCompressor lzma`, not `/SOLID`**: a solid datablock is assembled uncompressed and the payload is ~2.9 GB, past the 2 GB an NSIS datablock can hold (`Internal compiler error #12345: error mmapping datablock`). Per-file compression puts the limit on the COMPRESSED total instead: ~1.2 GB, which also has to stay under the 2 GB of a GitHub release asset. makensis compresses on one core (NSIS 3.13 has no multithreaded mode): about 7 minutes.
  - The welcome page doubles as the runtime-requirements notice (VC++ always; the Adrenalin driver for AMD, the NVIDIA driver for NVIDIA; Vulkan needs nothing): a backend whose driver is missing is skipped silently at runtime, so the installer is the one place the user is told.
  - Three visible components: binaries with the bundled runtimes (required), PATH addition, Start Menu shortcut. No dependency checkboxes: there is nothing left to fetch.
  - Installs `bin\` (llama.cpp binaries, the GPU runtimes, `llama-cpp-config.exe`, `llama.ico`, the uninstaller), `lib\` and `.kpack\`, leaving **no file at the install root**. The uninstaller is `bin\llama-cpp-uninstall.exe`, namespaced because `bin\` may be on the SYSTEM path. The closing `RMDir "$INSTDIR"` is **non-recursive on purpose** (`$INSTDIR` is user-chosen); anything left at the root is never removed and keeps the directory alive, so the Uninstall section carries an explicit list of legacy root files, and an entry may only be retired once no installed version can still have written it.
  - **Upgrade path**: `CheckPreviousInstall` runs the old uninstaller with `_?=$INSTDIR` (suppresses its copy-to-`$TEMP`, so `ExecWait` can wait, but then it cannot delete itself), so the caller removes it after `ExecWait` returns, both the new path and the legacy one. Installers up to 1.16 did not carry cuBLAS and **parked** the downloaded pair in `$INSTDIR\cublas-parked\` across the old uninstaller's `RMDir /r "$INSTDIR\bin"`; `SecMain` now deletes a park dir left by a cancelled upgrade, since the package ships its own copy. The uninstaller still leaves `cublas-parked\` alone: a downgrade to such an installer parks there before running THIS uninstaller.
  - What an older install left OUTSIDE `$INSTDIR` is not touched: a ROCm dist its helper extracted under `C:\TheRock\<version>` (~25 GB), with the machine `HIP_PATH` and PATH entry naming it. Harmless to the new install (its own `bin\` precedes PATH) and the user's to delete.
  - **No custom config page**: runtime configuration is `llama-cpp-config`'s job. Start Menu shortcut: "llama.cpp Config" → `llama-cpp-config.exe`.


===== tools/AGENTS.md =====
# tools\

Diagnostics and the 1.x → 2.0 config migration, not part of the build pipeline or the installer.

## migrate-config-v1-to-v2.ps1 / .sh

The offline migration of a 1.x config to 2.0, run by hand (`-DryRun` / `--dry-run` shows the changes): the binary carries no migration code, by decision, and 2.0 simply ignores the moved server.ini keys. Twins (Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\llama.cpp\config`, Linux `~/.config/llama.cpp`, plus a copy out of `/etc/llama.cpp` when the new directory is empty): keep their rules in step. What moves, each written as the launch 1.x actually ran:

- `LoadMode` (or the legacy `Mlock`/`NoMmap` pair: both true = `mlock`, `NoMmap` alone = `none`) → `load-mode` in every preset, replacing the preset's own, because 1.x put `-lm` on the router line ALWAYS (auto when unset). Under auto only a disagreeing preset key is rewritten; with none of the three keys in server.ini the step is skipped.
- `Device` → `device` in every preset, and `TensorSplit` → `tensor-split` with it: a server-wide device list won over the preset's and took its split along, so with a `Device` and no `TensorSplit` the preset's own `tensor-split` is DROPPED (its weights were positional over another list). A `TensorSplit` alone is written alone.
- `OverrideTensor` → `override-tensor` in every preset, overwriting the preset's own: the server-wide rules REPLACED a preset's rather than adding to them.
- `SplitMode` → `split-mode` in every preset, only when it is `none`/`tensor`/`row`: a server-wide `layer` was dropped on read.
- `Fit` → `fit = on|off` in every preset, replacing the preset's own: 1.x always put `-fit` on the router line, `off` unless `Fit = true`.
- `PrefillAssistant = false` → `prefill-assistant = false` in every preset (1.x passed `--no-prefill-assistant` to every model); `true` was llama.cpp's default and writes nothing.
- `CacheReuse` above 0 → `cache-reuse` in every preset, replacing the preset's own (1.x put `--cache-reuse` on the router line).
- `MmprojDevice` → `mmproj-device` only where the preset has an `mmproj` and no device of its own (the env `MTMD_BACKEND_DEVICE` it rode loses to a preset key).
- Then the keys, their commented placeholders and the comment block above each go from server.ini. Both `presets.ini` and `presets.ini.disabled`; each touched file is backed up as `<file>.v1.bak`; line endings and UTF-8 without BOM are kept.
- **No moved key left in server.ini means already migrated, and the script exits untouched.** Without that guard a second run reads the absence as 1.x's `auto` and rewrites every preset's `load-mode`.

## report-memory.ps1

Reads only `%LOCALAPPDATA%\llama.cpp\`, so it runs on an installed machine. Slices the memory of the **currently running** llama-server across llama-server's load log (every buffer's size and device → the *requested* footprint) and Windows' `GPU Process Memory` counters (dedicated VRAM vs shared system RAM actually handed out), and cross-checks them: an oversubscribed device does not fail to allocate, WDDM silently pages the excess over PCIe and the only symptom is slow decoding. Tensor placement is an **`of which tensor override`** sub-row under `model weights`, never a row of its own and never summed (an override *moves* a tensor, it allocates nothing extra). The host section names the pinned share of the model weights (the shared-GPU-memory number). `-Json` for machine output.

Placement sources: the **rules** come from the ROUTER's `spawning server instance with args:` block (the already-merged winner, not `presets.ini`/`server.ini`), which sits above the `device_info` banner and is matched to the child by its `[port]` prefix, never by proximity. The **hits** come from `tensor <name> (<n> MiB <type>) buffer type overridden to <buft>`, which is `LLAMA_LOG_DEBUG` (verbosity **5**), so at `-lv 4` no hits does not mean nothing matched; the report says which.

Parsing traps, all handled:

- **Which block**: matched to a live listener by PORT (`Get-LivePorts`; the log records no PIDs), never "the newest block". The `device_info` banner and `buffer size` lines are TRACE (`-lv >= 4`), so at `LogVerbosity` 3 the live child writes neither and the newest block belongs to a dead run. An older block is cut at the next block's banner, and the failure path names the cause (live port plus its `-lv`).
- `sched_reserve` is **re-emitted** for the draft context after the mmproj loads (clip ownership comes from the `reserve_compute_meta` source line, not a banner latch), and the compute graph is **re-reserved** when slots come up (collapse per `(owner, kind, device)`, do not sum). `sched_reserve` groups are assigned by group, not by the open context: a later group goes to whichever reference group (model's or draft's) its total is closer to.
- **Spill test**: shared memory beyond that backend's pinned `*_Host` buffers, never the arithmetic alone and never raw shared (the log accounts for no driver context, WDDM can trim other processes, and `free at load` is a snapshot). An uncorroborated overflow reads `AT THE LIMIT`, not paging; the same subtraction stops a host compute buffer charted under the card from reading as a spill. The threshold is **512 MiB**, clearing the driver's own non-local memory present even with free VRAM (~290-360 MiB on the 4070 Super/CUDA, ~210 MiB on the R9700/HIP).
- With `--fit on` the model is **loaded twice** (a `no_alloc` dry run, then the real one): collapse override lines by tensor name.
- `CPU_Mapped` (mmap'd weights) is **host memory, not a device**: match host on `^CPU`, not `^CPU$`.
- A **sliding-window model builds two KV caches** (`llama_kv_cache_iswa`) announced with the same line: latch on the `creating non-SWA / creating SWA KV cache` banner, or de-duplication eats one.
- A **separate draft/MTP head prints its own `print_info` block** after the model's: arch/params/file type/file size take the FIRST occurrence.
- The `buffer size` regex must be exhaustive (**model / KV / RS / compute / output / LoRA / DSV4 `<name>` state**): an unmatched kind vanishes from the balance sheet.
- A separate **draft FILE** loads after `loading draft model` but before its own `llama_context`: the banner is latched, or its weights are charged to the model and then de-duplicated away.

A rule ending in **`=CPU` does not mean ordinary RAM**: with a GPU backend up and mmap off, CPU-side weights live in that backend's *pinned* host buffer (`CUDA_Host`/`ROCm_Host`, charted as **shared GPU memory**); with mmap on, the same debug line is logged and the tensor is served from `CPU_Mapped`, leaving the pinned buffer at 0 MiB. So the pinned verdict is gated on a pinned model buffer actually appearing in the `buffer size` lines, and an `of which` **larger than its parent row** is suppressed and the mismatch reported.

## pcie-bench\

A host <-> GPU transfer micro-benchmark over the three paths of ggml-cuda's internal AllReduce (`allreduce.cu`), which `split-mode tensor` reduces through on Windows (no NCCL/RCCL; `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE` defaults to `internal` off Linux) by staging through pinned host memory, so the SLOWER card's link bounds tensor parallelism: DMA copies, 16-byte kernel stores/loads into mapped host memory, and the arrival-token handshake. One `pcie-bench.cpp` built twice by `build.ps1` into `build\windows\pcie-bench\`: as HIP (ROCm's clang as CXX + `hip::device`, clang 24 or newer, the same route as `02-build.ps1`) and as CUDA through `pcie-bench.cu`, which only `#include`s it (nvcc from the newest CUDA dist, `Find-Dist`). It also writes the `compile_commands.json` VS Code's IntelliSense reads; CMake Tools is kept from configuring it (`.vscode\settings.json`). The HIP exe gets the dist's runtime trio beside it (load order, as the installer). Purpose: seeding `GGML_CUDA_AR_COPY_THRESHOLD` / `GGML_CUDA_AR_COPY_CHUNK_BYTES` and measuring a slot's real bandwidth, which Windows does not expose for a card behind a switch. HIP-on-Windows traps it encodes: DMA copies are timed wall-clock (event timing reports impossible figures), and the handshake flushes the stream (`hipStreamQuery`) after the launch, since a kernel does not start until a later API call submits it. Reference numbers: `pcie-bench\README.md`.


===== benchmarks/README.md =====
# benchmarks

`bench.db` (SQLite, committed as a binary blob) holds the measurements behind the
`docs\*.md` guides with everything needed to reproduce or compare them: the machine
(CPU, RAM amount, type and speed, GPUs with VRAM and driver, OS and kernel), the software
stack (llama.cpp tag, build and commit, framework version, ROCm, CUDA and Vulkan SDK dists,
the `patches\llama.cpp\` applied), the model file, every parameter of the run (all the
preset keys, `server.ini`, environment variables) and the prompts, stored in full.

`schema.sql` is the data model, with a comment per column. The unit is the **run**: one
model load with one configuration, inside a **campaign** (the question a set of runs
answers, and its answer). Its **results** are the requests sent to it (or the llama-bench
repetitions); `run_device` holds the memory per device. Only runs that finished are
recorded: a run that failed to load, crashed or was cancelled is left out. A run carries
its start and end as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS UTC`; the first campaigns, recorded by hand,
have the start date only.

## Recording

The configurator compiles `schema.sql` in and records every finished run of its
Benchmark tab and of `bench sweep` into a WORKING database under its data directory
(`~/.local/share/llama.cpp/bench/bench.db`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\llama.cpp\bench\bench.db`):
one campaign per tab run or sweep, machine and software detected (the ROCm, CUDA and
Vulkan SDK dist versions are the ones llama.cpp was built against). Credentials and
service URLs are never stored, and an absolute path is stored as its file name, in the
logs and reports too: the file is public with the repo. A campaign the docs cite is
copied here, with its answer:

```sh
llama-cpp-config bench answer sweep-20261016-181500 "4 wins up to 40k, 3 beyond"
llama-cpp-config bench publish sweep-20261016-181500 --to benchmarks/bench.db
```

`publish` copies whole campaigns and everything their runs point at (the machine matched
by a fingerprint over its description, not by id), the engine's logs and the report
included, and refuses a campaign already here. To record straight into this file instead,
point the configurator at it: `BenchDb = <clone>/benchmarks/bench.db` in its
`settings.ini`. Before committing, check the file for credentials (`strings bench.db`).

## Querying

`llama-cpp-config bench query` reads the working database, or this one with
`--db benchmarks/bench.db`, read-only:

```sh
bench query campaigns                                  # question, answer, runs per campaign
bench query models                                     # models tested, runs and campaigns each
bench query machines                                   # CPU, RAM (size, type, speed), GPUs, OS
bench query summary --model 27b --show ctx-size,cache-type-k
                                                       # per run and prompt (or llama-bench test):
                                                       # prefill and decode mean +- sd, draft acceptance, free VRAM
bench query runs --gpu r9700 --param no-host=true      # runs that computed on the R9700 with no-host
bench query results --prompt long-43k --llama v0.7.0   # one line per request
bench query run 3                                      # everything about one run
bench query diff 1 3                                   # the parameters that differ
bench query sql "SELECT ..."                           # anything else
```

Filters: `--campaign`, `--model` (name, file or quant), `--gpu` (a GPU the run computed on,
`run_gpu`), `--device` (the preset's device list), `--llama`, `--status`, `--since`,
`--param K=V` or `K~V` (contains), repeatable. `--format csv` or `json` for other tools,
`--full` for whole text cells.

## Reading with sqlite3

```sh
sqlite3 -header -column benchmarks/bench.db \
  "SELECT campaign, label, prompt, rep, prefill_tps, decode_tps FROM v_result ORDER BY campaign, label"
```

`v_result` joins each result with its run, campaign, model and llama.cpp version;
`v_run_params` gives a run's parameters per scope as one line.


===== patches/llama.cpp/README.md =====
# Out-of-tree patches applied to the llama.cpp working tree

`02-build.ps1` applies every `*.patch` in this directory to `$cfg.LlamaCppDir`
(`build\windows\llama.cpp`) in file-name order, right after it detaches the clone onto
the newest `vX.Y.Z` release tag, and **reverts them again before the next
fetch/checkout** so the checkout always runs on a clean tree (a tag bump that
touches a patched file would otherwise abort with "local changes would be
overwritten"). Anything the reverts leave behind (an older revision of a
patch that has since changed, a hand edit) is **stashed** in the clone, never
discarded: `git stash list` there has it back. Both legs probe with `git apply --check` first, so re-running
the build is a no-op rather than an error, and a patch that no longer applies
**fails the build fast** instead of being skipped silently: an out-of-tree
change that quietly stops being applied is a binary that behaves differently
from the one the last build produced, with nothing in the log saying so.

Patch files are stored **verbatim** as published upstream, never reflowed or
"improved": a refresh is then a plain diff against the new upstream file, and
the checked-in sha256 below is the one the publisher serves. `.gitattributes`
pins `*.patch` to LF for the same reason (a CRLF checkout of the patch does not
apply to LF sources).

The llama.cpp clone lives under the gitignored `build\`, so nothing here is
committed into it; the patch is re-applied from this directory on every build.

## `0001-qwen35-fastmtp-d2t.patch`

Teaches the `qwen35` arch (Qwen3.5 / Qwen3.8) to read a **trimmed draft
vocabulary** from a standalone MTP head GGUF, which is what HauhauCS's
"FastMTP" sidecar ships. Source:

- <https://huggingface.co/HauhauCS/Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-HauhauCS-Aggressive-MTP-GGUF/resolve/main/HauhauCS-FastMTP-llama.cpp.patch>
- sha256 `981285400b59dc45cf99936b6ff66d4b3aa0f1b532f85fa51418cb407e51d615`
- published against upstream `4df29be4f4c3673f428170fda944a5b19f743bb8`;
  verified to apply and compile clean on tag **b10488** (2026-08-20)
- **refreshed locally for v0.6.0** (2026-10-05), so the checked-in file is no
  longer the publisher's bytes: v0.6.0 inserted an optional `cls_out`
  projection between `output` and its NULL fallback in `load_arch_tensors`,
  which broke the first hunk's context. The publisher had not re-cut the patch
  (the URL above still served the sha256 above), upstream still has no `d2t`
  in `qwen35.cpp`, and `git apply --3way` re-applied it with every added and
  removed line unchanged; only offsets and context moved.
- **refreshed again for v0.7.0** (2026-10-16), and this time ONE added line
  changed: #30097 replaced the file's local `mtp_only` with the shared
  `nextn_flags()` helper, so the `d2t` gate reads
  `(nf.trunk & TENSOR_NOT_REQUIRED)` instead of `mtp_only`. Same condition
  (the helper probes the same `blk.0.attn_norm.weight` under the same
  `n_layer_nextn > 0`). The last hunk also lost its trailing
  `ggml_build_forward_expand(gf, cur)` context line: #30445 dropped that call
  from `graph_mtp` (the caller expands `t_logits`), which the patch never
  touched. Upstream still has no `d2t` in `qwen35.cpp`.
- **hardened locally** (2026-10-16): the publisher's patch trusts `d2t`, and
  `ggml_set_rows` writes wherever an index points, so a crafted sidecar could
  write past the logits tensor. The patch now refuses at load a `d2t` that is
  not I32/I64, has no entries or more than the vocabulary, and, through a
  `load_tensors` override (`models.h`) that runs once the data is in, any entry
  outside `[0, n_vocab)`. Checked-in sha256:
  `34db7d82eeb458a6c1e31ec80c2a83c047b484c7b6d2792be32cf75a109c5c91`

**What it changes.** A FastMTP sidecar is an MTP-only GGUF (no trunk blocks)
whose LM head covers **32,768** rows instead of the target's 248,320, plus a
`d2t` tensor (I64) mapping those rows onto target token ids. Stock
`src\models\qwen35.cpp` sizes `output.weight` as `{n_embd, n_vocab}` and so
rejects the file at load with `expected 5120, 248320, got 5120, 32768`. The
patch reads `d2t`'s length as `n_vocab_out`, sizes the head to it, and, in
`graph_mtp`, scatters the draft logits back onto the full vocabulary
(`ggml_fill` to `-INFINITY` then `ggml_set_rows` through `d2t`) so the sampler
and the verify step see ordinary full-width logits.

**Blast radius.** Both legs are gated on `model.d2t`, which is created only for
an `mtp_only` file that actually carries a `d2t` tensor. A normal target GGUF
with embedded MTP heads, and every other arch, take byte-identical paths. `d2t`
itself is not new: it is an existing `llama_model` member that `dflash.cpp` and
`eagle3.cpp` already load and scatter through in exactly this shape; this patch
is that same code on the third arch that needed it.

**The one divergence from the upstream copies is load-bearing, not sloppiness.**
`dflash.cpp` and `eagle3.cpp` both assert `model.d2t->type == GGML_TYPE_I64`
before the scatter; this patch accepts I32 as well, and it has to. The
FastMTP sidecar ships `d2t` as **I32** (it is the file's single `i32` tensor),
which `ggml_set_rows` accepts (`ggml.c`: `c->type == GGML_TYPE_I64 ||
c->type == GGML_TYPE_I32`) but the upstream assert would reject. So do not
"restore" that assert when refreshing the patch: it would turn the one file this
exists for into a hard failure. The load-time checks above (type, length, every
index in range) replace it.

**Confirming it is live** (rather than assuming): the loader logs
`QWEN35 MTP using d2t draft-vocab trim (n_vocab_out = 32768)`, at llama.cpp's
INFO level, which llama-server prints only from `-lv 4` up (`LogVerbosity` in
`server.ini`); at the usual 3 the line is absent and proves nothing. The failure
mode without the patch is a refused load
(`expected 5120, 248320, got 5120, 32768`), not a slow or silently
non-speculating server, so a server that starts at all with the sidecar attached
is already evidence.

Verified end to end on b10488 (2026-08-20) against
`Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-HauhauCS-Aggressive-Q6_K_P.gguf` +
`...-FastMTP-32K.gguf`: the trim line appears, an 8-token completion comes out
coherent, and llama-server reports `draft acceptance = 0.57143 (4 accepted /
7 generated), mean len = 2.33`, i.e. the drafts are real and accepted rather
than garbage the target quietly rejects.

**Using it from llama-cpp-config**: a per-model preset with
`model-draft = <...FastMTP-32K.gguf>`, `spec-type = draft-mtp` and
`spec-draft-n-max = 3` (the depth the publisher qualified; the stock embedded
head runs at 2). Because the sidecar is a **separate draft file**, unlike the
embedded MTP heads, `device-draft` / `n-gpu-layers-draft` are honoured here and
should pin the sidecar to the same device as the model. `--spec-draft-p-min`
has no preset field; llama.cpp's default applies.

**When to drop it.** If upstream lands the same support, `git apply --check`
starts failing and the build stops with a message pointing here: delete the
file (git history keeps it) and rebuild. Re-verify at every llama.cpp tag bump
that touches `src\models\qwen35.cpp`; a refreshed patch from the publisher
replaces this one only with the `d2t` validation carried over (or present in
it), and the sha256 above changes with it.


===== .agents/skills/optimize-preset/SKILL.md =====
---
name: optimize-preset
description: Tune one model's section of presets.ini for speed (prefill and decode t/s) and GPU memory (VRAM used, headroom), measuring every change with llama-cpp-config bench sweep and recording it in bench.db. Use when asked to optimize, tune, speed up, fit, or shrink a preset, to raise its context, to rebalance tensor-split / n-cpu-moe / override-tensor across GPUs, or to pick KV cache type, ubatch-size or a speculator for a model.
---

# Optimize a preset (speed and VRAM)

Goal: for ONE preset, find the configuration that is fastest on the user's real workload
while leaving a safe VRAM margin on every card, and prove it with measurements recorded in
bench.db. Never claim a number you did not measure; never apply a value that only loaded.

## 0. Read first

- `docs/AGENTS.md` and the guides it lists: the card guides (`docs/amd_ai_pro_r9700.md`,
  `docs/nvidia_rtx4070_super.md`) and the model guide if one exists
  (`docs/qwen3.8_*.md`). Their "Settings" tables are the measured optimum per key, their
  "look better than they measure" tables are the refuted levers, their placeholder
  tables are the open questions. Start from these, do not re-derive them.
- The memory notes (VRAM headroom, per-model tuning, workload): they hold the last
  measured state of each preset and the levers still untested.
- `llama-cpp-config/AGENTS.md` "Footguns": semantics of `tensor-split` (normalized over
  blocks + output layer), `override-tensor` (regex matched anywhere, first rule wins,
  anchor with `^` and `$`), `load-mode`, `parallel` (divides the context; absent means
  auto 4 slots), the embedding table left in pinned host RAM, `spec-type` vs MTP heads.

## 1. Establish the baseline

```bash
llama-cpp-config preset list                 # ids, model paths, switched-off ones
llama-cpp-config preset show <id>            # the section as INI
llama-cpp-config server show                 # Threads, ThreadsBatch, env knobs
llama-cpp-config bench query campaigns --db benchmarks/bench.db
llama-cpp-config bench query summary --db benchmarks/bench.db --model <name> \
  --show ctx-size,cache-type-k,n-cpu-moe,tensor-split,ubatch-size
```

Config lives in `~/.config/llama.cpp/` on Linux, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\llama.cpp\config\` on
Windows. **Back up before any edit**: `cp presets.ini presets.ini.bak-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)`.

Ask the user (once, only what is unknown):
- the minimum acceptable context (`ctx-size`) and slot count (`parallel`);
- the workload. Default assumption on this machine: conversations grow gradually, so
  rank by **decode deep in the context** (the long prompt, ~40k tokens) and short-increment
  prefill; cold full-prompt prefill is secondary.

Record the baseline: one live run with the long prompt and one with the short prompt,
plus the memory readout of section 3. Without a baseline there is no "faster".

## 2. Levers, in the order they usually pay

Use the model's facts (Model info in the Models tab, or the GGUF header: layers,
experts, attention interval, MTP heads, `token_embd` quant) to skip levers that cannot
apply.

1. **Placement (VRAM, biggest decode effect).** Everything on the GPUs beats anything in
   RAM. Dense: `device` + `tensor-split` (layer split; the last device gets output head,
   drafter logits and sampling: give it the card with spare VRAM). MoE: lowest
   `n-cpu-moe` that fits, then fine balance with partial `override-tensor` moves
   (`^output\.weight$=<dev>`, single `blk\.N\.ffn_*_exps` tensors). Keep the device order
   explicit; dropping `device` can reorder the cards.
2. **KV cache type.** `f16` decodes faster deep in context and has smaller compute
   buffers; `q8_0` only to reach a context `f16` cannot fit. It fits ~66% of q8_0's
   context, not half: measure, do not compute.
3. **`ubatch-size`.** Prefill only (+5% from 512 to 1024 on dense), decode unaffected; it
   costs compute buffer (~0.5 MiB per ubatch token per device). On MoE with experts in
   RAM a larger ubatch is paid in experts moved off the GPU: usually not worth it.
4. **Speculator.** `draft-mtp` if the GGUF has MTP heads; otherwise `ngram-map-k`
   (size-m 64, size-n 12 measured best). Sweep `spec-draft-n-max`. Read per-rep numbers:
   a speculator changes the output path.
5. **MoE extras.** `no-host = true` with `op-offload false` (+9% prefill), `load-mode`
   per the guides, `moe-cache-mib` off.
6. **Server-wide** (`server set`, affects every preset; ask first): `Threads` =
   `ThreadsBatch` = physical cores.

## 3. Measuring VRAM (do this for every candidate)
