A load-only check is not enough: the CUDA/HIP pools grow at the first real prefill
(~40 MiB on the 4070, ~85-170 MiB on the R9700). Read memory **after a long-prompt
prefill**:

```bash
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,memory.used,memory.free --format=csv   # use memory.free: ~406 MiB is driver-reserved
for d in /sys/class/drm/card*/device; do [ -r $d/mem_info_vram_used ] && \
  echo "$d used=$(( $(cat $d/mem_info_vram_used)>>20 ))MiB total=$(( $(cat $d/mem_info_vram_total)>>20 ))MiB gtt=$(( $(cat $d/mem_info_gtt_used)>>20 ))MiB"; done
grep -E 'model buffer size|KV buffer size|RS buffer size|compute buffer size' \
  ~/.local/share/llama.cpp/logs/llama-server.log | tail -30
```

Windows: `tools\report-memory.ps1` (requested vs actual, spill into shared memory).

Margins to keep (free memory after the prefill):
- R9700 / ROCm: >= ~500 MB. Below ~50 MB hipBLASLt runs out and the child hangs (kill -9).
- RTX 4070 SUPER / CUDA: >= ~300 MiB `memory.free` is comfortable; ~30 MiB has worked
  but `cudaGraphInstantiate` OOM has been seen at ~400 MiB with ngram-mod. Tell the user
  the margin when it is below ~500 MiB and let them choose.
- GTT growing above idle on an AMD card means spill into system RAM: a regression.

## 4. Measuring speed: one key per sweep

```bash
llama-cpp-config bench sweep --preset <id> --key <key> --values '<a>,<b>,<c>' \
  --reps 3 --prompt-file ~/.config/llama.cpp/bench-prompt-long.txt \
  --campaign <model>-<key>-<yyyymmdd> --question "<what this answers>"
```

- `--values` with commas inside a value (`device`, `tensor-split`): separate with `;`,
  and end a single such value with one (`'16,50;'`): without a `;` the spec splits on
  `,` and sweeps `16` and `50`. `unset` removes the key for a leg. Ranges: `2..8`.
- Live mode restarts llama-server per leg and sends `cache_prompt: false`, temp 0.
  Synthetic mode (llama-bench) needs the server DOWN and cannot model the drafter,
  `ctx-size` or `parallel`: use it only for raw prefill/decode of placement or quant.
- Two keys that must move together (e.g. `n-cpu-moe` + `tensor-split`): set one by
  editing presets.ini, sweep the other; or run one sweep per combination into the same
  campaign. Restart (`llama-cpp-config control restart`) after every manual edit: the
  router reads presets.ini only at start.
- `--apply original` (default) leaves the preset as it was; apply the winner yourself
  after the memory check. After an interrupted sweep, restore with `--restore-to` (the
  value is in `~/.local/share/llama.cpp/bench/sweep-pending-*.json`).
- Differences under ~3% are noise unless sd says otherwise; re-run in reversed order
  (thermal drift lands on the last leg).
- A leg that does not load or OOMs is a result too: note the boundary (e.g. "180224 f16
  does not load").
- Swapping `device` order changes which card takes the output head, MTP head and
  sampling, so the mirrored `tensor-split` usually does not fit: sweep a few splits per
  order (one campaign) and compare each order at its best split with a safe margin.
- A sweep restarts the server per leg, so one readout after it shows only the last leg:
  run a background sampler of free memory per card (the section 3 commands every 2 s,
  into a file) and take each leg's minimum after its load phase.

Read results:

```bash
llama-cpp-config bench query summary --campaign <campaign> --show <key>
llama-cpp-config bench query diff <run-a> <run-b>
```

(Add `--db benchmarks/bench.db` when `settings.ini` `BenchDb` does not already point at
the repo's database; on this machine it does.)

## 5. Safety rules

- **Warn before the first live run**: a benchmark request replaces the slot's
  conversation, and the next restart with `SaveStateOnShutdown` on overwrites that
  model's `.llamastate` snapshot. Get an OK.
- Never `pkill -f <pattern>` with a pattern that appears in your own command line; use
  `llama-cpp-config control stop|restart`.
- Never drop or reorder `device` on a multi-GPU preset without meaning to.
- `spec-type = draft-mtp` on a model without MTP layers fails to load.
- One preset at a time; do not touch other presets or server.ini without asking.

## 6. Apply and record

1. Present a table: candidate, prefill and decode (short and long prompt, mean +- sd),
   free VRAM per card after prefill, context reached. Recommend one; the user decides
   when the trade is speed vs margin or context.
2. Apply the chosen values to presets.ini (back up first), restart, run one confirming
   long-prompt measurement and the memory readout.
3. `llama-cpp-config bench answer <campaign> "<conclusion with the numbers>"`, and
   `bench publish <campaign> --to benchmarks/bench.db` when the runs went to the
   working db. Scan for secrets before committing: the db is public.
4. Update the guides in the same change: the row in the model/card guide's settings
   table with "(campaign `<name>`)", fill or retire the placeholder it answers, no
   absolute paths, no em-dashes. Per-card guides stay about their card (no `CUDA0`,
   no split vector of one pairing).
5. Update the memory note for that model with the new preset, the numbers, the backup
   file name and the levers still untested.
6. Update this skill (section 7) before closing the task.

## 7. Keeping this skill current

This file is the procedure, and every optimization run is a chance to correct it. Before
reporting the result, review what the run taught about the METHOD and edit this file
(`.agents/skills/optimize-preset/SKILL.md`; `.claude/skills` is a symlink to the same
file) in the same change as the guides.

Update it when the run:
- used a command, flag or check that worked and is not here (a new `bench` subcommand or
  option, a better VRAM readout, a log line that tells a fact faster);
- found a step here wrong, stale or missing: a command that changed or failed, a margin
  that proved too small or too large, an ordering of levers that misled;
- found a new lever that pays, or refuted one listed in section 2 (keep the lever's
  numbers in the guide; here only the rule and where the evidence lives);
- hit a new failure mode or safety trap (an OOM pattern, a hang, a key combination that
  fails to load);
- changed with a new llama.cpp release or configurator version (a renamed key, a removed
  flag, a new default).

What goes where:
- **Here:** how to optimize, valid for any model and machine: steps, commands, checks,
  rules of thumb with the regime they hold in, traps.
- **The guides (`docs/`):** measured values of a card or a model, with their campaign.
- **Memory:** this machine's state: the current presets, backups, what is still untested.
  A fact that is only true of one preset never goes here.

How to edit:
- Change the section the lesson belongs to; do not append a log or changelog (git
  history is the changelog). Rewrite or delete what the run proved wrong instead of
  adding a contradicting line.
- Keep it terse and generic: cite a guide section instead of copying its numbers, keep a
  number here only when it is a threshold the procedure acts on (a VRAM margin, a noise
  level).
- Verify a command with `--help` or by running it before writing it down.
- Repo rules apply: no em-dashes, no absolute paths outside the per-user config and data
  directories already named here, no secrets. Keep the frontmatter `description` in step
  with the sections when the scope changes.
- In the final report, list what changed in the skill and why (one line each), or say
  that the run taught nothing new about the method.


===== llama-cpp-config/AGENTS.md =====
# llama-cpp-config\

Rust GUI + CLI configurator; five tabs:

- **Server** → `server.ini`.
- **Models** → `presets.ini` (an on/off switch per preset MOVES its section, verbatim, to `presets.ini.disabled`, which llama-server never reads; only the Models tab and the CLI's `preset` commands see both files: `presets.rs` module header).
- **Benchmark** → measure or compare presets into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\llama.cpp\bench\bench.db`, or the database `settings.ini` `BenchDb` names (`db::work_path`; e.g. a clone's `benchmarks/bench.db`) (`bench\db.rs`: the repo's `benchmarks\schema.sql` compiled in, one campaign per tab run or sweep, a run written in ONE transaction only when its preset finished, never a failed or cancelled one; `bench\record.rs` holds it in memory meanwhile; `bench\host.rs` detects machine and software, the ROCm/CUDA/Vulkan SDK dist versions coming from `build.rs` via `LLAMA_FW_*`; `bench\query.rs` is `bench query`/`answer`/`publish`; secrets are dropped and absolute paths cut to file names ON THE WAY IN (`public_value` for a value, `public_text` for logs, notes and reports), since the database may be the public one; the machine row is matched by a fingerprint over a canonical text, never by id) (the live engine's prompt is a FILE, see below; its unattended half is the CLI's `bench sweep`, which runs the engines once per value of ONE presets.ini key, records each finished leg as a run of one bench.db campaign with the digest as its report, and restores the key (a `sweep-pending-<stamp>.json` marker holds the value to restore until it is back); a live leg RESTARTS llama-server since the key rides the child's launch args; `;` separates values because `device`/`tensor-split` contain commas).
- **Integrations** → `opencode.json` model list (every ENABLED preset; once Save has created the provider, every preset change and server.ini save rewrites it: `integrations.rs` module header) + Claude Code snippet.
- **Settings** → the configurator's own preferences.

Plus llama-server run controls, the View-logs window and the HW Monitor window (`hwmon.rs` + `gui\hw_window.rs`: live bars for every GPU, CPU, memory and the models directory's disk; the sampler is per OS, `hwmon\linux.rs` and `hwmon\windows.rs`, and a bar added to one should be weighed for the other). Architecture, per-tab walkthrough, UI-binding conventions, source layout and test topology live in [README.md](README.md), and every module has a `//!` header; read those first. The crate has zero Clippy warnings; it uses `#[cfg]` compile-time branching for OS portability in `paths.rs`, `gui.rs`, `runstate.rs`, `main.rs`, `gguf\ffi.rs`, and `proc.rs` (the shared `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` process launcher used by `devices.rs` / `server_version.rs` / `runstate.rs`); `single_instance.rs` is Windows-only wholesale. `cargo test` links only thanks to the `/force:multiple` rustflag in the generated `.cargo\config.toml` (see [docs\build-pipeline.md](../docs/build-pipeline.md), lld).

## Common changes

The two recurring recipes live as authoritative checklists at the top of the module they start in; follow those, not a from-memory list:

- **Adding a per-model preset field** fans out to 7 places (kebab-case INI key ↔ snake_case Rust field; INI schema → form struct → widget → conversions). Checklist: top of `src\presets.rs`. Trace an existing field like `ctx-size` as the template.
- **Adding a server (server.ini) field** is the same shape plus **three spots in `cli.rs`** (the server has a per-field `set`; the preset CLI is list/show/delete/enable/disable, with no per-field `set`) plus **`runstate::server_args`** (the launch/Command-Line leg). Checklist: top of `src\server_cfg.rs`.

Cross-cutting rules the recipes rely on:

- **Before adding a new property, field, or function, search the codebase for existing ones that already cover the case** (e.g. `base_url_default` on `ServerForm` already tracks whether the Base URL was user-specified; a duplicate `chat_url_specified` on `AppState` was a mistake). Grep first, then decide if something genuinely new is needed.
- **Model Info rows show facts, not explanations.** No trailing hints like "saves VRAM (slower)" or "`--reasoning on|off` switches it". The doc comments (`///`) explain the why; the displayed value is just the fact.
- **`open_chat_url` prefers Base URL custom over launch URL.** `state.slint` derives it from `chat_url` when `server_form.base_url_default` is false (custom Base URL), otherwise from `launched_url`, falling back to `chat_url`. The form's `base_url_default` field is the single source of truth: no duplicate property needed.
- **Model info names only files the preset LOADS.** `draft_line()` reports the model's own MTP heads plus the preset's `--model-draft` ("draft file: x.gguf", whose header is the "Draft file" row below), and "none" when there is neither, never a blank. Do not reintroduce a "best match" drafter line based on file names: it reads as a claim the drafter is in use, and a name-token match offers drafters built for a different fine-tune. Choosing a drafter belongs to the Assets picker.
- Editable widgets bind **two-way** (`<=>`) to `AppState.…`; a one-way binding goes stale after the first edit. The sanctioned one-way exceptions (read-only displays, `for`-row widgets, `MappedComboBox`, the Slider/AutoSlider push, `SegmentedControl`) are catalogued in the README's conventions section.
- Nearly every step is guarded by a test that fails when skipped: INI and form round-trips (`presets.rs` / `form.rs`; `server_cfg.rs` / `server_form.rs`), the CLI leg's exhaustive-struct tests (`cli.rs`), the launch-arg leg (`runstate.rs`), widget-binding staleness (`src\tests\ui_bindings.rs`: one widget PER KIND, plus the one-way-binding text lint `src\tests\binding_lint.rs`) and the Models/Integrations callback funnel (`src\tests\save_flow.rs`). The unguarded remainder: forgetting the widget entirely (the field never appears in the UI), and skipping the path-field `validate_for_save` step (a `;`/`#` path then saves fine and reloads truncated; see the checklists' final step).
- Run the e2e tests with `cargo test` (debug profile), **not** `--release`: they need Slint element debug info that `build.rs` emits for non-release builds only. Bare `cargo test` is the right command: the generated `.cargo\config.toml` already caps both thread pools at `BuildJobs`. Full test-topology rationale (single shared `#[test]`, `LLAMA_CPP_CONFIG_DATA_ROOT` redirect, `src\tests\` vs `tests\`): README "Tests" section.

## Footguns

Recurring traps, each with a canonical write-up in the code:

- **On Wayland a window that was never shown still exists** (`gui.rs` `discard_unshown_wayland_window`): Slint's winit backend creates the native window of every component when the event loop starts, and Wayland has no hidden windows, so a tray-only launch (`gui --minimized`) and the always-hidden log window were toplevels the compositor knew about, and GNOME's dock listed the app as running. `hide()` alone is a no-op on a window never shown; the helper waits for the native window, then calls `show()` + `hide()` in one event-loop turn (the backend destroys a Wayland window on hide), before any frame is drawn. Any new window that starts hidden needs the same call. The tray icon has such a window too (`AppTray` is a root, so the backend builds one for it; it showed in GNOME's Alt+Tab as an empty "Slint Window"), and no `window()` to call the helper on: `DiscardTrayWindow`, a winit application handler installed before the first component, does the same show + hide on the first event that window receives, recognizing it by the backend's default title: the program's name since Slint 1.18 (`default_window_title`, mirroring i-slint-core's `application_name`; "Slint Window" before, and a Slint upgrade that changes it again brings the ghost back). So every real window must set a `title`. Related: `slint::set_xdg_app_id("llama-cpp-config")` must run AFTER the first component is built (it fails with no platform) and names the desktop entry the deb/rpm install, which is how the dock finds the icon instead of a generic gear. Check either with `WAYLAND_DEBUG=1`: `get_toplevel`, `set_app_id`, `xdg_toplevel.destroy`.
- **`--mlock` / `--no-mmap` are ONE enum, `-lm`/`--load-mode`**, a per-preset key (`presets.rs` `load_mode`, canonicalized by `server_cfg::normalize_load_mode`; blank = llama.cpp's `auto`): the old flags are removed upstream (a launch line carrying one fails to parse and nothing starts). The modes do not compose like two bools: `mlock` alone is mlock **without** mmap (`use_mmap` is true only for `mmap`/`mmap+mlock`/`auto`, `llama-model-loader.cpp`). Hence one dropdown. It used to be server.ini `LoadMode`, always on the router's command line and so shadowing every preset; the router line now carries no `-lm`, `--split-mode` or vision-device setting, and nothing in the binary migrates an old `server.ini` (its `LoadMode`/`Mlock`/`NoMmap`/`SplitMode`/`MmprojDevice` keys are ignored).
- **GPU placement is a table, not two text fields** (`gpu_split.rs` + `GpuSplitTable`, Models tab only: `device`, `tensor-split`, `split-mode` and `override-tensor` are per-preset keys, because server-wide they rode the router's CLI and shadowed every preset's, so server.ini has no GPU distribution): `--tensor-split` is positional over `--device`, so neither means anything alone. A blank tensor-split over 2+ GPUs = split by *free VRAM*, not evenly. The vector is **normalized** over `n_layer_all + 1` positions (every block plus the **output layer**), so on a 33-block model 83/84/85% are one launch. Hence `Layout`/`block_counts`: where the model is known the column is edited in **blocks**, and since a vector summing to the positions assigns exactly itself, writing counts back is the identity (`29,5` *is* blocks 0-28 / 29-32+output). A count cannot be edited alone (rows absorb from the back), so the cell is a **`Slider`** committing on `released` (`SpinBox` is banned for stray wheel events; the accessibility set-value action raises `changed` without `released`). The INI still stores the **ratio**, re-derived per model, so re-pointing a preset rescales it.
  - The `--split-mode` combo sits ABOVE the table, and the columns follow it. `layer`: blocks. `row`: the raw ratio (matrix bytes follow row fractions; CUDA/ROCm only, Vulkan silently falls back to the layer cut, KV follows the layer cut); deprecated upstream in favour of `tensor` but still parses, so listed last and labelled. `tensor` (experimental tensor parallelism): the same raw ratio, the checked devices become ONE meta device and every weight AND the KV cache are cut by that vector; a blank vector is an EVEN cut there. `none`: llama.cpp keeps only `devices[main_gpu]`, so the head row reads 100%, other checked rows "unused", and the drag handle is the only control left. A strip under the combo (`preset_split_mode_warning`) names `tensor`'s launch-time refusals: an arch on `llm_arch_supports_sm_tensor`'s deny-list (mirrored in `gguf::arch_supports_sm_tensor`, re-check on a llama.cpp bump), `flash-attn = off` (`auto` is promoted), and NO device pinned (it then pools every detected device, duplicate Vulkan views and iGPU included); `--fit` is merely skipped, so `ctx-size` is the user's to size. The single "layer (default)" entry merges default/layer (same launch everywhere).
- **"All layers offloaded" still leaves the embedding table in PINNED HOST RAM** (`tensor_override.rs` + `TensorOverrideTable`): `token_embd.weight` stays in a host buffer, pinned with a GPU backend active (`ROCm_Host`/`CUDA_Host`), which Windows reports as **Shared GPU memory**. The cure is an `override-tensor` rule (`^token_embd\.weight=ROCm0`); the value is split on `,` and at the first `=` before parsing and cannot be escaped, hence a table.
- **A tensor-placement regex is matched ANYWHERE in the name, and the first matching rule wins** (`tensor_override.rs` `KINDS`, `tensor_picker.rs`): `output\.weight` also takes every `blk.N.attn_output.weight` and `token_embd\.weight` takes `per_layer_token_embd.weight`, so the families outside the blocks are anchored with `^`, and everything the picker writes ends in `$`. The picker and the coverage lines match with the `regex` crate, which agrees with llama.cpp's `std::regex` (ECMAScript) on what the table writes but not on every hand-written form (no lookaround or backreferences): a regex it cannot read opens the picker unticked and says so, never guesses. `compile` checks its output against the tensor list before returning it; keep that check, it is what makes a tick mean exactly that tensor.
- **…but that cure is CONDITIONAL, and violating either condition costs 10x** (`gguf.rs` `embd_line` / `embd_pin_warning`, surfaced as Model info → **Embeddings** + a warning strip): (1) `token_embd`'s **own** quant must have a GPU `get_rows` kernel (`ggml-cuda.cu` `supports_op`), or the lookup round-trips to the host every token (measured 25 → **2.8 tok/s**; prefill untouched; GPU engines read 0%, so it looks *idle*, not slow). Today every K- and IQ-quant has one (IQ4_NL/MXFP4 only for widths that are multiples of 256; Vulkan's set is a superset, so the mirror errs toward a needless warning there); what still trips the warning is Q8_1, Q8_K, TQ1_0/TQ2_0 and NVFP4. The file NAME does not tell you the type (Unsloth's `Q6_K_XL` keeps `token_embd` at Q8_0 while `Q4_K_XL`/`Q5_K_XL` drop it), so the row reads the header. (2) The card needs **room**: pinning MOVES the table into VRAM (~1.26 GiB at Q8_0), which can tip a full card into paging (23 → **13.7 tok/s**). Prefer the **per-preset** scope. `gpu_has_get_rows` mirrors the upstream switch and must be diffed against CURRENT upstream source on every bump: a missing type costs a needless warning, a type listed *without* a kernel green-lights the pin this exists to prevent.
- **The image encoder is placed on its own** (`presets.rs` `mmproj_device`, the Models tab's "Vision GPU"): it defaults to the FIRST entry of `--device` (a preset's `device` key counts; `--device none` keeps it on CPU), falling back to the first GPU backend when no device is pinned. The per-preset `mmproj-device` key (`-mmdev`) overrides both defaults.
- **Preserving past thinking is ONE flag, not a template kwarg** (`presets.rs` `reasoning_preserve` + the README): `--reasoning-preserve` sets `preserve_reasoning`, which `caps_apply_preserve_reasoning` (`common/jinja/caps.cpp`) expands into THREE template variables (`preserve_thinking`, `clear_thinking`, `truncate_history_thinking`) because templates disagree on which they read. Hand-writing one into `chat-template-kwargs` is a **silent no-op** on templates reading another, and never registers with the capability probe. "Supported" only means the trace showed up in the probe's render: a template that preserves *unconditionally* (Ornith-1.0) reads as supported while gating on nothing. Upstream defaults it ON: the tri-state's `default` (`None`, pass nothing) means ON on every supporting template, `off` (`--no-reasoning-preserve`) is the one value that changes anything, and a hand-written `preserve_reasoning` kwarg draws a DEPRECATED warning.
- **`--parallel` DIVIDES the context, and omitting it is not "1 slot"** (`integrations.rs` `effective_ctx`, surfaced as each opencode model's `limit.context`): `n_ctx_seq = n_ctx / n_parallel` (`llama-context.cpp`), so `parallel = 2` halves it, **unless the key is absent**: the default `-1` = auto picks 4 slots *and a unified KV cache*, under which every slot sees the whole `n_ctx`. And `ctx-size` absent means the model's **trained** context. Publishing 256k for a 256k model that pins `parallel = 2` promises a context llama-server rejects at 128k+.
- **Thinking support is read from the template's SOURCE, and the verdict is about the TEMPLATE** (`gguf.rs` `Thinking` / `thinking_line` / `past_thinking_line`, Model info → **Thinking** + **Past thinking**): there is no Jinja engine here, so the scan looks for the names the flags act on (`enable_thinking` for `--reasoning`; `preserve_thinking`/`clear_thinking`/`truncate_history_thinking` for `--reasoning-preserve`): a `yes` is a fact, a `no` only "no marker this build knows" (MiMo-VL's template names nothing yet the model thinks). **Past thinking** tells what llama-server's log cannot: its probe prints `supports preserving reasoning` both for a template that gates on the flag and for one that preserves unconditionally.
- **The FOLDER a drafter sits in does not say which speculator it needs** (`gguf.rs` `draft_spec_type`, applied in `gui\models_tab.rs`'s `draft_picked` handler): `mtps\` and `dflashs\` share one dropdown, and `draft-dflash` vs `draft-dspark` share arch, name shape and folder, told apart only by a `markov_w1.weight` tensor INSIDE the file. The wrong one does not fail: `is_dspark` changes the drafting batch layout (`common/speculative.cpp`), so every draft is rejected: speculation that costs and never pays. Hence the picked FILE's header decides, mirroring `common_speculative_types_from_gguf`. The framework still WRITES the key (shows the verdict before launch, works on older builds). The folder guess is the FALLBACK: a separate MTP head with `block_count = 0` (gemma4-assistant) matches no rule, and an unreadable GGUF is not evidence.
- **Draft placement needs a draft FILE** (`presets.rs` field docs): `--device-draft` / `--n-gpu-layers-draft` are read only inside `if (has_dft())`. With MTP heads embedded in the main GGUF, the draft context runs on the target's device, ignoring both. With a SEPARATE head file they apply: pin model **and** draft to one device, or leave the draft on CPU (multi-device "auto" fitting crashes those heads). An EMPTY `--device-draft` inherits the model's `--device` list (`common_base_params_to_speculative`), and a draft on exactly one device is forced to the layer split even under an inherited `--split-mode tensor`.
- **BF16/F16 on gfx1201 can die inside hipBLASLt, and the knob is an env var no llama.cpp flag can reach** (`server_cfg.rs` `rocblas_use_hipblaslt` → `runstate.rs` `env_vars`, Server → **SDK Tuning** → ROCm): when the dist's rocBLAS/hipBLASLt run against the DRIVER's System32 `amdhip64_7.dll`, the **second** 16-bit GEMM in a process fails at kernel launch and llama-server aborts on the first prefill batch. The root cause is the runtime mix, not hipBLASLt: with the dist's own runtime beside the exe (bundled into `bin\` by `03-package.ps1`) every dist passes under default routing, so an installed tree needs no knob; a build from source still runs the driver's runtime. `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` (Tensile) is the full-speed fallback. It rides the **environment** (rocBLAS reads it below llama.cpp; hence `env_vars`, not `server_args`) and is a **tri-state** defaulting to *unset*, since hipBLASLt is the faster path where it works: `Some(false)` and `None` are opposite instructions. rocBLAS parses an int: export `"1"`/`"0"`, never `"true"`/`"false"`. An upstream "fixed in X" proves nothing unless it says which runtime the test loaded. Upstream: llama.cpp #25836, TheRock #7271.
- **`split-mode tensor` across a CUDA and a ROCm device ABORTS at load, and the cure is an env var** (`server_cfg.rs` `cuda_allreduce` → `runstate.rs` `env_vars`, Server → **SDK Tuning** → Tensor-split AllReduce; `gpu_split::mixes_cuda_and_rocm` feeds the Models tab's mode strip): ggml-cuda and ggml-hip are one source and declare ONE backend guid, so each backend's `ggml_backend_is_cuda` accepts the other's device, the direct AllReduce comes up over the mixed pair and the first reduction dies with `ROCm error: invalid argument` (v0.6.0, under the default and under `internal` alike). `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=none` hands the reduction to the meta backend's generic path and the model loads (on an R9700 + RTX 4070 Super: half the layer split's prefill, the same decode). One variable for BOTH backends, server-wide, inert outside tensor mode; an unknown value is run as `none` behind a warning, hence the closed list (`ALLREDUCE_MODES`, mirrored by `Options.allreduce_modes`, e2e-asserted). `nccl` means RCCL on ROCm (linked by the Linux build only) and NCCL on CUDA. Re-check the guid clash on a llama.cpp bump: when upstream fixes it the strip's clause and the hint's last paragraph go.
- **The speculative widgets are DISABLED, not cleared, so a dead `spec-type` survives invisibly and kills the model** (`presets.rs` `prune_inactive_draft_keys`, `form.rs` `prune_inactive_draft_fields`, clone boundary in `gui\models_tab.rs`): the speculative controls are `enabled: draft_active` (`state.slint`), greyed out (never blanked) when the model embeds no MTP heads and no draft file is picked, so a value arriving from elsewhere is written straight back by `form_to_preset`. Spec type is the exception: it stays enabled, offering only `none` and the `ngram-*` types without a draft (they draft from the context), and the prune keeps those entries of the list (`presets::spec_type_is_draftless`); a `draft-*` entry the combo cannot show reads as `none` until the prune removes it. Ways in: **Clone** copies every field onto a different model (`..base.clone()`, straight to disk), and re-pointing a preset at another model keeps the old keys. `spec-type = draft-mtp` on a model without nextn layers makes `llama_init_from_model` return nullptr (`context type MTP requested but model doesn't contain MTP layers`) and **the model fails to load**. Hence the prune runs where the model becomes known (`update_model_info`, leaving the preset dirty so Save is reachable) and where a preset reaches disk without a form (clone, save), gated on having actually READ the GGUF. The verdict is per-FILE (`nextn_predict_layers` in the header), as upstream reads it for every arch (the holdout, granite-switch, is refused with the same log line).
- **The flag that can save a 17-minute prefill rides the ROUTER's command line, and reaches the children by its flag name** (`slot_state.rs` module header, `runstate.rs` `server_args`, Server tab → **Conversation snapshots**): prompt reading is QUADRATIC and the KV cache is process memory, so every restart re-reads the whole conversation (a 234k-token prompt: ~17 minutes, against ~8.3 GiB of sequential IO through `/slots/{id}?action=save|restore`). Those endpoints exist only under `--slot-save-path`. It does reach the router's children: `get_map_key_opt` (`common/preset.cpp`) maps options by FLAG NAME as well as env name, so the router's argv becomes `base_preset` and `preset.merge` copies it onto every model (`unset_reserved_args` does not prune it). It had no `LLAMA_ARG_*` name before v0.7.0 (`LLAMA_ARG_SLOT_SAVE_PATH`, #30272), which is why the framework never relied on one. Since v0.7.0 the dump also carries the context checkpoints (#26004) and the draft state (#30574), so it outgrows the KV-only figures above. The directory must EXIST before launch (validated while PARSING the flag; a missing folder stops the server), so `start()` creates it and the flag is emitted only when the toggle is on. The restore is useful only because `SERVER_TASK_TYPE_SLOT_RESTORE` also restores `slot->prompt.tokens`, which `get_common_prefix` compares against.
- **`llama-bench` takes no prompt and no temperature, and a comma in its flags means SEPARATE benchmarks** (`bench.rs` module header + `synthetic_argv` / `synthetic_sweeps`, the Benchmark tab's **synthetic** engine): `-p` is a token COUNT and it never samples (a stronger reproducibility guarantee, hence a second engine). INI translation: `device` and `tensor-split` are `,` in `presets.ini` and `/` for llama-bench; `override-tensor` is `,` in the INI and `;` there (llama-bench splits `-ot` on `,` into configuration GROUPS first), so `54,12` would silently become two benchmarks. llama-bench runs the CARTESIAN product and `-d` multiplies pp tests too, so prefill and decode are separate invocations (6 tests instead of 12). It cannot model the speculative drafter (`tg` reads ~HALF the live rate with MTP on), the mmproj, the chat template, `--ctx-size` or `--parallel`; `bench::caveats` prints that list under Run and in the saved report.
- **The two benchmark engines demand OPPOSITE llama-server states, and the live one must disable the prefix cache** (`bench\exec.rs` module header, `bench::live_body`): live needs the server up; synthetic needs it DOWN (a second resident copy spills into shared memory over PCIe and reads as a backend regression), so the tab stops it via `gui::stop_server_async_with` (never a bare `runstate::stop`: that path also writes the conversation snapshot and keeps footer, tray and the run-status generation counter in step, or the next tick reports the stop as "no longer running" in red). Live sends `cache_prompt: false` (otherwise repetitions hit the prefix cache and the mean is fiction); `timings.cache_n` is read back as a row note. The request streams with `timings_per_token` (`bench::parse_stream`) so decode splits at the reasoning/answer boundary (thinking drafts poorly, answers well, so one mean describes neither); the jsonl keeps both phases plus a 64-token trace per repetition. Upstream ask: ggml-org/llama.cpp#29372 (a `reasoning` object in `timings`); if it lands, read it instead of streaming. Each preset is committed to bench.db as it finishes (a TDR at the third preset must not lose the first two); a failed preset is dropped and its error carries the engine's last lines. So comparisons can be FALSIFIED: llama-bench's row is kept **verbatim** beside the aggregated point; the resolved **flash-attn is read back** and a disagreement between compared presets is warned ABOVE the table (`auto` resolves per BACKEND; the tab does not pin it); an **environment block** records boot time, each adapter's driver version and every device id's NAME (ids are not stable across driver states). Legs are NOT interleaved, so thermal drift lands on whichever ran last: re-run in reversed order when a difference is small.
- **The live benchmark's prompt is a FILE** (`bench.rs` module header + `normalize_prompt` / `ensure_prompt_file` / `prompt_report_rows`, `gui\bench_tab.rs`, `settings.rs` `BenchPromptFile`): the tab carries a PATH, never a text, so the Run button and `bench sweep` send the same bytes. **Two** prompts ship compiled in and are **seeded** on first use: `config\bench-prompt.txt` (`DEFAULT_PROMPT`, 230 chars, the default) and `config\bench-prompt-long.txt` (`LONG_PROMPT`, from `assets\`, ~40k tokens). Seeded, not installed, because `bin\` under Program Files is not editable without elevation and upgrades overwrite it; `ensure_prompt_file` only ever CREATES and picks its seed by FILE NAME. The long one exists because a benchmark ranks settings only in the regime it measured (at 40k the ranking moves). It is a FROZEN snapshot, pinned to LF in `.gitattributes`, **never regenerated when the docs it came from change** (see `assets\README.md`). **Edit** opens the system's `.txt` handler (a Slint `TextEdit` is sluggish at a few hundred KB); the readout refreshes within one 5 s tick, gated on `(path, mtime, len)`. `on_bench_run` re-reads the file ITSELF, never the display cache. The report identifies the prompt by path + size + **sha256** (`src\sha256.rs`, hand-rolled to avoid six crates), text embedded only up to 4096 chars; the same three facts go into the jsonl header. `normalize_prompt` strips a leading BOM and folds CRLF, so the digest does NOT match `Get-FileHash` on a CRLF file, which the report says.


===== llama-cpp-config/README.md =====
# llama-cpp-config

GUI + CLI configurator for [llama.cpp-framework](..).

```text
llama-cpp-config                  → launch the GUI
llama-cpp-config gui              → same, explicit
llama-cpp-config control start    → start llama-server
llama-cpp-config control stop     → stop llama-server
llama-cpp-config control restart  → restart llama-server
llama-cpp-config control stop-and-close → stop llama-server and close the GUI
llama-cpp-config server show      → print server.ini
llama-cpp-config server set ...   → update server.ini fields
llama-cpp-config preset list      → list models with their presets
llama-cpp-config preset show ...  → print one preset
llama-cpp-config preset delete .. → remove one preset
llama-cpp-config preset disable . → switch one preset off (enable: back on)
```

## GUI

Built with [Slint](https://slint.dev). The nav rail switches between five tabs (Server, Models, Benchmark, Integrations, Settings) and carries the server run controls (Start/Stop over Open chat UI over HW Monitor over View logs over Refresh) at its bottom, reachable from any tab. A status footer shows the llama-server state (running / not running) and version.

The non-obvious run controls:

- **Open chat UI** launches the URL the RUNNING server was started with (`AppState.launched_url`, snapshotted at launch: a save while running changes the file, not the listening process; the footer says "restart llama-server to apply"). When the GUI didn't start the server it falls back to the SAVED Base URL (`AppState.chat_url`, from `ServerConfig::opencode_base_url_or_default()`: uses the custom Base URL if set, otherwise auto-derives from host+port; a `0.0.0.0` bind maps to `localhost`, since a listen address isn't a connectable one).
- **Window sizes are remembered.** The configurator, the View-logs window and the HW Monitor window each record their size in `settings.ini` (`MainWindowSize`, `LogWindowSize`, `HwMonitorWindowSize`, as `<width>x<height>` in logical pixels) when they close and at Quit, and open at that size on the next launch; a maximized window is not recorded, and an empty key means the default size. The size reaches the window through its `initial_width`/`initial_height` properties, which the .slint binds its preferred size to (`gui.rs` `RememberedSize`), because the winit backend re-applies the preferred width on every show and would undo a plain `set_size`.
- **HW Monitor** (Linux and Windows; the button is absent elsewhere) opens an independent, non-modal window (`HwWindow`) with one card per device and one horizontal bar per metric, refreshed once a second while it is open: every GPU (GPU, VRAM, and what spills out of VRAM: GTT on amdgpu, Shared memory on Windows; Memory activity where the driver reports it; PCIe RX/TX on NVIDIA), the CPU, memory (RAM, page cache, swap on Linux; RAM, Cached, Committed on Windows) and the disk holding `ModelsDir` (read, write, busy). No helper process is involved. On Linux amdgpu, CPU, memory and disk are files under `/sys` and `/proc`. On Windows the adapters come from D3DKMT and their usage from the GPU performance counters Task Manager shows (any vendor, no vendor library), the CPU from `GetSystemTimes`, memory from `GetPerformanceInfo`, the disk from `IOCTL_DISK_PERFORMANCE`; none of it needs elevation. NVIDIA is on both the driver's NVML (`libnvidia-ml.so.1`, `nvml.dll`), loaded at runtime (absent driver, absent card); on Windows an NVIDIA card NVML does not answer for still gets the counters' bars. Each device is headed by a checkbox (ticked by default): unticked, the device's card is hidden and nothing is read for it until it is ticked again; the choice is kept in `settings.ini` (`HwMonitorHidden`). A rate has no natural 100%, so the PCIe and disk rate bars are drawn against the peak seen since the window opened, which the readout names. The source of every bar is tabled in `src\hwmon.rs`'s header.
- **View logs** opens `logs\llama-server.log` in an independent, non-modal window (`LogWindow`) that follows the file live like `tail -f`, leaving the main window fully interactive while it is open. Auto-scroll (checkbox, on by default) keeps the view on the last line; untick it to read scrolled-back text while the server keeps writing: the view then stays exactly where it is (new lines only extend the scrollbar), and ticking it again jumps back to the end. The log is a list of rows, one per line, not a text box: a line (or part of one) is selectable and Ctrl+C copies it, and **Copy all** puts every line the window holds on the clipboard. A header readout ("17.3 MB · updated 5 s ago") ages every tick, so a live-but-quiet tail (llama-server goes silent between requests) reads differently from a stuck one. Always enabled: the log outlives the process, so it's exactly what you want after a crash or failed start. The same window opens from the tray menu's **Open log** (`gui\tray.rs` invokes the same `view_logs` callback, so it is one viewer, not two), without surfacing the configurator. The tray menu lists **Open chat UI** (enabled while the server runs; `AppState.open_chat`, the rail button's own path), **Open HW Monitor** (where the button exists) and **Open log** in the rail's order, under **Open configurator**. The tail mechanics (a worker thread that polls every 500 ms and lives only while the window is open, so no file IO runs on the UI thread; a bounded number of retained rows; truncation/rotation handling) live in `gui\log_window.rs` and `ui\log_window.slint`. The rows are a virtualized `ListView` on purpose: a `TextEdit` lays its whole text out again on every append and every frame, which froze the window for most of each poll once the tail reached a few tens of KB. Rotation is real, not just tolerated: when llama-server stops and the log is larger than the Settings tab's threshold (`LogRotateKb`, 1024 KB by default; `LogRotate` off keeps one growing file), `runstate::stop` files it away as `llama-server-<yyyymmdd-hhmmss>.log` (closing instant, UTC) and leaves a blank one, so the window reads as empty after a stop and the next run starts clean; a run that died without a stop is filed at the next start under its last-write time, and the newest 10 closed logs are kept (`runstate.rs` module header).
- **Refresh** (the button, or F5) re-reads `server.ini` / `presets.ini`, re-scans the models directory, reloads integration state, and re-probes the run status, llama-server version, and GPU devices (the exe can change under us, e.g. a `02-build.ps1` rerun). Use it after adding a model file or hand-editing a config file outside the GUI, without restarting.

Each tab body is its own Slint component (`ui\server_page.slint`, `ui\models_page.slint`, `ui\bench_page.slint`, `ui\integrations_page.slint`, `ui\settings_page.slint`). All shared state (every property and callback the pages and Rust both touch) lives in a single `export global AppState` (`ui\state.slint`); the pages read/write `AppState.<name>` directly and Rust drives it via `app.global::<AppState>()`. `ui\app.slint` is just the window chrome (nav rail, run controls, modals, footer) plus the tray.

Modal dialogs (New/Clone picker, Rename, the Delete confirmation, the discard-confirm guard, and the chat-template preview) overlay the whole window: Esc or a backdrop click dismisses them, and the app-wide shortcuts (Ctrl+S, Ctrl+N (which jumps to the Models tab first), Ctrl+1–5, F5) are inert while any one is open (`AppState.modal_open`). The New/Clone picker is keyboard-completable: when the filter narrows the list to exactly one model, Enter picks and confirms it.

Any navigation that would replace a dirty form (switching presets, Refresh/F5, opening New…/Clone…/Rename…) first asks to discard the unsaved edits (`gui::confirm_discard_then` parks the action Rust-side until the verdict). A preset's on/off switch is deliberately not one of them: it moves the preset between two files without changing a word of it, so it keeps the form as it is (see the [Models tab](#models-tab-presetsini)).

### Server tab: `server.ini`

- Fields: port, bind addresses (one checkbox per address: localhost, 0.0.0.0 and each detected IPv4 interface; several may be checked and ride `--host` comma-separated, except 0.0.0.0, which is exclusive), load mode, threads, cache reuse, threads-batch, models-max, models-dir (with a Browse button). **threads** and **threads-batch** are sliders capped at the machine's logical-processor count, each with a "default" checkbox that omits the flag (let llama.cpp pick). The scalar fields (port, cache reuse, models-max) are `DefaultLineEdit`s: a "default" checkbox omits the flag, or an explicit integer (see [Numeric fields are text](#numeric-fields-are-text-never-a-spinbox) for why they are not SpinBoxes).
- **Advanced** card: **Web UI MCP proxy** (`--webui-mcp-proxy`, on by default: the bundled chat UI needs it to call MCP tools) and **Log level** (`-lv`). They round-trip through `server.ini` and default to the framework values above when untouched.
  - **Log level** is a **dropdown, not a number**: `-lv` has exactly six levels (`OUTPUT:0`, `ERROR:1`, `WARN:2`, `INFO:3`, `TRACE:4`, `DEBUG:5`; `common/arg.cpp`), a message prints when its own level is ≤ the threshold, and a free-number field could only ever offer levels that do not exist. The framework default is `TRACE:4`; `DEBUG:5` is what also logs the per-tensor `buffer type overridden` lines that `tools\report-memory.ps1` reads. The form carries the *label*, and `server_form::LOG_LEVELS` is asserted equal to `Options.log_levels` in the e2e so the two lists cannot drift.
- **SDK Tuning** card, the one card that holds no llama.cpp flags: environment variables of the GPU vendor's own libraries, exported onto llama-server at launch (`runstate::env_vars`, so the Command Line card renders them too) and inherited by the router's per-model children. It is split per SDK because a knob here is read *below* llama.cpp and is inert on every other backend; llama-server never sees it, so nothing validates it either. **CUDA** carries nothing today. **ROCm** carries **hipBLASLt** (`ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT`), a tri-state: `default` never exports the variable and leaves rocBLAS its own routing, `on` forces hipBLASLt (`=1`), `off` forces Tensile (`=0`).
  - `off` is the one that pays, and only on **gfx1201** (Radeon AI PRO R9700) under ROCm/TheRock 7.14: rocBLAS routes 16-bit GEMMs through hipBLASLt there, and the **second** 16-bit GEMM in a process fails at kernel launch; f32 is immune, and the identical call sequence passes on Tensile (standalone repro: default and `=1` fail, `=0` passes across batched/strided/plain × bf16/f16; a single visible GPU changes nothing). llama-server aborts on the first prefill batch of any BF16/F16 model. Forcing Tensile is a cure, not a trade: pp512 587 t/s / tg128 35 t/s measured on Ornith BF16, where the older `GGML_CUDA_CUBLAS_COMPUTE_TYPE=f32` workaround managed ~19 t/s prefill. Upstream: llama.cpp #25836, ROCm #6461, TheRock #7271.
  - What actually fails is a MIX, and the second cure follows from it: Windows resolves a static import from the exe's own directory and then System32, both before PATH, and the AMD driver keeps its own `amdhip64_7.dll` in System32, so llama-server runs the dist's `rocblas.dll` + `libhipblaslt.dll` against the DRIVER's HIP runtime rather than the dist's. Put the dist's runtime next to the exe and the same GEMMs pass on ROCm 7.14.0, 10.0.0 and the 10.1 nightlies alike, `on` included, so hipBLASLt itself is healthy and no ROCm bump was ever going to fix this. `03-package.ps1` does exactly that (it bundles the dist's whole runtime into `bin\`), which is why an installed tree can leave this knob at `default`; a build from source still runs the driver's runtime.
  - The card also carries the **ROCm availability warning**, which is not a setting but the only place a missing HIP backend is ever mentioned. ggml loads backends dynamically and drops one whose imports don't resolve **without a word**, so a machine whose ROCm dist is absent, half-extracted, or simply not on the child's PATH does not fail: the same AMD card keeps working through Vulkan, slower, with nothing saying why. The verdict comes from the device probe, never from a file list (`devices::rocm_warning`): an AMD GPU present with no `ROCm*` device beside it *is* the diagnosis, and it holds whatever ROCm version is installed. Only the *hint* inspects the runtime (llama-server's own directory when the installer bundled it there, else the dist `HIP_PATH` names), and it checks two landmarks that survive a version bump, `bin\hipblas.dll` and `bin\rocblas\library` (a fuller file list would be wrong: `origami.dll` ships in TheRock 10.0.0 and not in 7.14.0, so requiring it would report a healthy 7.14 install as broken). The three hints name three different remedies: nothing installed, installed but incomplete, or complete and therefore a launch that isn't reaching it (most often a missing or outdated Adrenalin driver, or on a build from source a console whose PATH still names a dist directory that has since been renamed). The second one is worth its own row because it fails LATE: with `rocblas\library` missing the backend loads and the device enumerates, then the first matrix multiply dies with `Could not initialize Tensile host`.
  - It is a tri-state and not a checkbox for the usual reason (`--flash-attn`'s shape, and the same `form::tri_state` / `tri_bool` pair): "don't export the variable" is a third instruction, and it is the framework default; the bug is per-arch, and hipBLASLt is the faster path everywhere it works. The CLI reaches all three as words (`server set --rocblas-use-hipblaslt default|on|off`), which is also the only way to clear it back.
  - **Tensor-split AllReduce** (`CudaAllreduce`, env `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE`, `server set --cuda-allreduce default|nccl|internal|none`) is the card's multi-GPU knob: which AllReduce `--split-mode tensor` reduces through. ggml-cuda and ggml-hip are one source and read the SAME variable, so it is one value for both backends, server-wide (the env reaches every router child) and inert in the other split modes. Unset (`default`) leaves the backend its own choice: on Linux the collective library the build links (RCCL in ggml-hip, NCCL in ggml-cuda), then the internal AllReduce; on Windows the internal one. It is a closed list (`server_cfg::ALLREDUCE_MODES`, normalized on read and again in `env_vars`) because the backend does not refuse an unknown word: it warns and runs it as `none`. `none` is also the ONE value under which a tensor split mixing a CUDA and a ROCm device loads: the two backends declare the same guid, each takes the other's device for its own, and the direct AllReduce aborts on the first reduction (`server_cfg.rs` `cuda_allreduce` has the measurement). The Models tab says so on such a preset (`gpu_split::mixes_cuda_and_rocm` -> `preset_mixes_cuda_rocm` -> the last clause of `preset_split_mode_warning`). Both knobs of the card are dropdowns (`EnumComboBox` over `Options.tri_states` / `Options.allreduce_modes`).
- **Conversation snapshots** card: `SaveStateOnShutdown` + `StateDir`. Dumps each loaded model's KV cache to disk when the server stops and pushes the newest one back on the next start, which buys back the one cost no amount of VRAM tuning touches: reading a prompt is **quadratic** in its length and the cache is *process memory*, so a restart re-reads the whole conversation from zero. Measured on a 234k-token prompt (Qwen3.8-27B, hybrid `qwen35`, 17 of 65 blocks carrying attention): the marginal cost per token fits `0.95 ms + 0.0287 ms per 1k of context`, i.e. 984 t/s at position 4k against 142 t/s at 218k, which integrates to roughly **17 minutes** of prefill, against ~8.3 GiB of sequential IO to reload the same state. Off by default: the disk cost is real, stopping waits for the write, and the next start loads the model eagerly to push the state back in, so a machine running short prompts pays all of that for nothing.
  - **How the flag reaches the child**, which is the part that nearly made this impossible. The `/slots` endpoints only exist on a server started with `--slot-save-path`, and the framework always launches the **router** (`--models-preset`), whose per-model children are separate processes with their own command lines. `--slot-save-path` had **no env name** upstream until v0.7.0 (`LLAMA_ARG_SLOT_SAVE_PATH`, #30272), and none is needed: it propagates because `get_map_key_opt` (`common/preset.cpp`) maps every option by its **flag name with the dashes stripped** as well as by its env name: the router parses its own argv into `base_preset` (`load_from_args` then `common_params_to_map`, which keys off `opt.args`), `preset.merge(base_preset)` copies it onto every model, and `unset_reserved_args` does not prune it. One flag on the router's line therefore reaches every child.
  - **Why the restore is worth anything.** `SERVER_TASK_TYPE_SLOT_RESTORE` does not merely refill the KV buffer: it deserializes the token list saved alongside it into `slot->prompt.tokens`, which is exactly what the next request's `get_common_prefix` compares against. A restored slot is therefore indistinguishable from one the server prefilled itself, and the prompt is skipped. Without that half the dump would be a file nobody reads.
  - **The directory must exist before the launch.** llama.cpp validates `--slot-save-path` while *parsing* it (`fs_is_directory`, or it throws "not a directory"), so a missing folder does not degrade to "no snapshots this run", it stops the server from starting. `runstate::start` creates it, and the flag is emitted only when the toggle is on for the same reason.
  - Saving covers every model the router reports as `loaded` (never `loading`, which has no slot yet, nor `sleeping`, which has released its KV cache); dumps of models that are no longer loaded are pruned after a successful save, which is what keeps "the newest file" meaning "the model you were working on". Restoring deliberately takes **only** that newest dump: a dump is one conversation, restoring is an 8 GiB read plus a full model load, and the LRU would evict whatever a second restore pushed out anyway.
  - The path is overridable because the default sits under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, on the system drive, and these files are large. Size follows the tokens the slot actually **holds**, not `ctx-size`: the dump is the conversation's KV cache, not the allocated context. Measured 4,703,061,864 bytes (4.38 GiB) for a 127,579-token conversation on Qwen3.8-27B, which is exactly the arch's 36 KiB per token; a full 262k context would be ~8.3 GiB. Plan disk against that ceiling, but a short conversation costs proportionally little. Those are v0.6.0 figures: since v0.7.0 the file also carries the slot's context checkpoints (#26004) and the draft context's state (#30574), so a restored hybrid model keeps its rollback points and a restored drafter does not start empty, at the cost of a larger file (up to `ctx-checkpoints` copies of the recurrent state plus the draft's KV; not re-measured yet).
- A read-only, multi-line **Command Line** card shows the exact `llama-server` invocation **Start** would run, one `--flag value` per line joined with the shell's line-continuation character (`` ` `` on Windows, `\` on Linux) so you can paste it straight into a terminal. It auto-sizes to its content (no inner scrollbar).
- **Network** card: **Base URL** and **API Key** fields for integration endpoints (opencode.json + Claude Code). **Base URL** has a **Default** checkbox: checked = auto-derived from host+port, nothing written to server.ini; unchecked = editable override (useful behind a reverse proxy or LLM gateway). The stored value is the base without `/v1`; the suffix is appended automatically only for opencode.json's `baseURL`. **API Key** has a **No key** checkbox: checked = no key used, field disabled; unchecked = editable. These fields also affect the Open chat UI button URL.

### Models tab: `presets.ini`

Scans `.gguf` files under the SAVED `ModelsDir`'s fixed subfolders: `models\` (main models), `mmprojs\`, `mtps\`, `dflashs\` (`model_scan::Category::subdir`); a file sitting at the `ModelsDir` root is not scanned, and an unsaved Server-tab `ModelsDir` edit doesn't move the scans until saved (like every client-facing projection, they follow the file). Shows the presets table, and edits per-model settings.

Every row of the preset list carries an **on/off switch**. On means the preset's section is in `presets.ini`, the file `llama-server --models-preset` reads; off **moves** it, verbatim (comments and hand-edits included), to `presets.ini.disabled` beside it, which nothing but this app reads. So a preset switched off is still listed (dimmed, after the enabled ones) and still editable, but llama-server stops offering it at its next start (the router reads `presets.ini` once, at launch), OpenCode drops it at once (see the [Integrations tab](#integrations-tab)), and the Benchmark tab no longer lists it, since a preset the server does not serve cannot be run. The design choices, all in `presets.rs`'s module header:

- **A second file, not commented-out lines.** Commenting a section out in place was the other option, and it loses on the way back: the file would then hold two kinds of `;` line, the user's own notes and parked keys, that no reader can tell apart.
- **The switch is not a navigation.** It rebuilds the list (`gui::refresh_preset_list`) and leaves the form alone, so an unsaved edit survives it and its Save lands in whichever file now holds the preset (`presets::save` resolves the file per id). Saving a switched-off preset therefore keeps it off.
- **Ids are unique across both files**: New… and Clone… de-conflict against the parked ids too, and Rename refuses one, because switching a preset back on onto a namesake would overwrite one of the two.
- **Destination first, then the source.** A switch interrupted between its two writes leaves the preset in both files, never in neither, and it then reads as enabled (the copy llama-server serves) until the next switch replaces the stale twin.

The editor is grouped into cards by concern:

- **Assets**, the file pickers and speculator selection: model file (`--model`), MMProj (`--mmproj`; its **Vision on GPU** offload toggle and the per-image token bounds moved to their own **Vision** card below), and ONE draft-model dropdown that merges MTP heads (scanned from `mtps\`) and DFlash drafters (scanned from `dflashs\`), both feeding `--model-draft`, plus the spec-type dropdown. The pick policy lives in Rust (`apply_draft_pick` in `gui/models_tab.rs`, unit-tested), not in the `.slint` handler:
  - The spec type comes from the picked FILE's own GGUF header (`gguf::draft_spec_type`, mirroring llama.cpp's `common_speculative_types_from_gguf`): arch `dflash` carrying a `markov_w1.weight` tensor → `draft-dspark`, without it → `draft-dflash`, a `blk.<block_count - 1>.nextn.eh_proj.weight` head → `draft-mtp`. The folder it was scanned from (`mtps\` → `draft-mtp`, `dflashs\` → `draft-dflash`) is only the fallback, for a header that names none of the three (a blockless MTP head file like gemma4-assistant) or a GGUF that will not open. The folder cannot be the primary source because `dflashs\` holds both DFlash and DSpark drafters, and running one as the other does not fail: `is_dspark` changes the drafting batch (`n_draft` masked positions against `n_draft + 1`, anchor-first, ceiling `block_size` rather than `block_size - 1`), so the drafts are read from the wrong positions and get rejected.
  - Either way, when a GPU is selected the draft is pinned to it, the FIRST one if several (`device-draft` takes a single device, and a multi-device draft split is exactly what crashes MTP heads; an EMPTY `device-draft` would inherit the preset's `device` list, but the picker writes it anyway, so the pin shows), with `n-gpu-layers-draft = 99` (the shared "all layers" sentinel, `Options.all_layers` / `form::ALL_LAYERS`, equality test-asserted); otherwise it falls back to CPU (`n-gpu-layers-draft = 0`).
- **Model info** (read-only, between Assets and Hardware Config): reads GGUF metadata through llama.cpp's own reader (runtime-loaded `ggml-base.dll`, `libggml-base.so.0` on Linux, no reimplemented parser; see `gguf.rs`): dense vs MoE (+ expert counts), layer count, trained context, GQA shape, the **Memory** kind (attention / recurrent / hybrid, `gguf::arch_memory`, a mirror of `llm_arch_is_recurrent` / `llm_arch_is_hybrid` to re-check on a llama.cpp bump: a recurrent state cannot be rewound, which is when context checkpoints matter), quant, and whether it embeds MTP layers, plus whether a matching MTP/DFlash drafter is present. A **Chat template** row reports the embedded `tokenizer.chat_template` as `Jinja (embedded)` / `embedded (non-Jinja)` / `none` (the `{%`/`{{` heuristic mirrors llama.cpp's `common_chat_verify_template`), with a **Preview** button (shown only when a template is present) that opens the raw template text in a modal. An **Embeddings** row reports `token_embd.weight`'s OWN quant and size, which neither the file name nor the `Arch / quant` row tells you (Unsloth's `Q6_K_XL` keeps the embedding table at `Q8_0`; its `Q4_K_XL`/`Q5_K_XL` drop it to a K-quant), and it is what decides whether the Tensor placement table may pin that table to a GPU: the backend needs a `get_rows` kernel for that exact type, and a type without one round-trips the table to host RAM on every token (25 → 2.8 tok/s measured with a Q4_K table, the GPU sitting at 0%). That whitelist (`gguf::gpu_has_get_rows`, mirroring `ggml-cuda.cu`'s `supports_op`) covers every K-quant and IQ-quant, so the Unsloth pair above both pin fine, and what trips the warning is Q8_1, Q8_K, the ternary TQ types, NVFP4, and IQ4_NL/MXFP4 on a model whose width is not a multiple of 256. The size is the VRAM the pin ADDS to that card. The verdict itself is not repeated in the row: it surfaces as a warning strip on the [Tensor placement table](#tensor-placement-table), at the moment a rule actually pins it. For MoE models a **MoE layers** row shows how many layers carry experts (with a "saves VRAM (slower)" note), sizing the `--n-cpu-moe` field; dense models have no such row because their FFN lever, `--n-cpu-ffn`, is sized by the plain layer count. Two rows read that same template for **reasoning**: **Thinking** says whether it does any (and whether `--reasoning on|off` can switch it: that flag only sets the template variable `enable_thinking`, so a template that never names it thinks whatever you pick), and **Past thinking** says whether `--reasoning-preserve` is a *lever* on this model at all; see [Keep past thinking](#keep-past-thinking-vs-a-raw-preserve_thinking-kwarg) for why that is the useful question and why llama-server's own log cannot answer it. Both are read from the template's **source text** (this app has no Jinja engine; llama.cpp answers the same question by rendering the template), so the subject of every verdict is the *template*, not the model: `no marker in the chat template` does not prove the model can't think; MiMo-VL's template names nothing at all, yet the model emits `<think>` and llama-server's parser picks it out of the output. What the silence *does* prove is that neither flag has anything there to act on. A **MMProj** row (projector type, vision/audio modality, encoder depth, image/patch size) and a **Draft file** row (the drafter's arch/layers and, for DFlash, the trained `block_size` → the implied `--spec-draft-n-max` ceiling) appear when those are selected. Reads are synchronous and uncached; if `ggml-base.dll` can't be loaded the box shows "unavailable" and the load is retried on the next read (only success is memoized), so a DLL that appears later (e.g. a finishing `02-build.ps1`) is picked up without a restart.
- **GPU distribution** (directly under Model info): [the table](#gpu-distribution-table).
- **Tensor placement** (under GPU distribution), [the table](#tensor-placement-table) for `--override-tensor`: the exceptions to the card above, i.e. tensors that go on a device of their own whatever their layer does.
- **Hardware Config**, the remaining placement knobs: GPU layers, MoE CPU layers, MoE cache (`--moe-cache-mib`, llama.cpp v0.7.0+, experimental upstream: VRAM caching the experts kept in host RAM; it acts only on layers whose experts are ALL in RAM, and the model fails to load on a dense model, under split mode `tensor`, or below the experts of one token, so a dense model drops the key (`presets::prune_inactive_moe_cache`), Save refuses it beside `split-mode = tensor` (`validate_moe_cache_split`), and the Models tab's Save refuses a value under the preset's LOAD floor (`moe_cache.rs`: the budget is split across GPUs by `tensor-split` and within a GPU by host bytes, so the load floor is the cheapest group's `(n_expert_used + 1)` experts over its share, and the hint quotes a second, larger number at which EVERY group caches; with 2+ GPUs and a blank `tensor-split` llama.cpp weighs by free VRAM at load, and only the floor no split gets under is shown); llama-bench has no such option, so only the live engine measures it), Dense FFN CPU layers (`--n-cpu-ffn`: the dense twin of `--n-cpu-moe`, keeping the first N layers' `ffn_up/down/gate` on the CPU; on a MoE model it reaches only the dense layers such a model carries, never the experts), Op offload (`--op-offload` / `--no-op-offload`: a `default`/`on`/`off` tri-state, on upstream; `off` stops llama.cpp from running an operation on the GPU when its weights sit in host RAM, which otherwise copies those weights to the card on every prefill batch of 32+ tokens: 385 → 608 tok/s of prefill at 93.7k tokens on a MoE with 8 blocks of experts in RAM behind a Gen4 x4 link, decode unchanged, a no-op with every weight in VRAM), Repack (`--repack` / `--no-repack`, the same tri-state, on upstream: host weights of a type the CPU has an interleaved kernel for move into the `CPU_REPACK` buffer, but only without a GPU: with one, llama.cpp puts host weights in the GPU's pinned host buffer, which it picks before the repack types, so the key is a no-op there unless No host buffer is on; measured in the `moe_cache.rs` module header), No host buffer (`--no-host`, no negative form, so a plain on/off written only when on: host weights go to ordinary RAM instead of the GPU's pinned host buffer, which lets the CPU repack them and takes those layers out of the MoE cache, where the floor then falls back to the cheapest layer's need), draft device, draft GPU layers.
- The remaining cards group the runtime knobs by concern: **Resource / context** (ctx-size, parallel, batch/ubatch), **KV cache** (K/V cache-type, flash-attn, cache-ram, cache-reuse: off by llama.cpp itself on a model with an mmproj or a cache that cannot shift), **Chat / reasoning** (jinja, [prefill assistant](#prefill-assistant), reasoning, reasoning-format, [reasoning-effort](#reasoning-effort), [keep-past-thinking](#keep-past-thinking-vs-a-raw-preserve_thinking-kwarg), plus the [output split](#thinking-budget-vs-the-answer): thinking budget, budget message, max output), **Sampling overrides** (temp, top-k, top-p, min-p, repeat/presence penalty, plus `backend-sampling`: a `default`/`on`/`off` tri-state like flash-attn, running the sampler chain on the GPU that holds the logits; `on` is the only value that changes the launch, since the flag has no negated form, and llama.cpp falls back to CPU sampling silently when the chain holds a sampler without a backend kernel, and turns it off outright under a grammar, a thinking budget or split mode `tensor`), **Advanced** (chat-template-kwargs), **Vision** (the `--mmproj-offload` offload toggle plus image-min/max-tokens, the per-image token bounds only dynamic-resolution vision models read; all gated on an mmproj being selected; Qwen-VL warns at load it wants `--image-min-tokens 1024`), and **Speculative decoding** (last, one sub-heading per speculator: the **draft model** group, Draft n-max, Draft p-min, plus the draft context's own K/V cache types, see the note below on why they are not the model's; then one group per n-gram speculator with its own `--spec-ngram-<type>-*` tuning: Match length, Min draft and Max draft for `ngram-mod`, Lookup n-gram and Draft m-gram for `ngram-simple`, `ngram-map-k` and `ngram-map-k4v`, plus Min hits for `ngram-map-k4v` alone (llama.cpp parses `--spec-ngram-simple-min-hits` and `--spec-ngram-map-k-min-hits` too, but v0.6.0 acts on neither, so they are not preset fields); every group is always shown, an n-gram group enabled only when Spec type is that speculator, and the save drops the other types' keys, `presets::prune_unused_ngram_keys`). Draft p-min (`--spec-draft-p-min`) is a confidence floor that stops a draft at its first unsure token; the verify batch is nearly free to lengthen on a bandwidth-bound card (halving it saved 5% per step on Qwen3.8-27B + DFlash2), so the floor only pays by cutting tokens that would have been rejected, and a high one loses (0.3 was a tie-to-small-gain there, 0.7 cost 10%). The field-by-field schema (each INI key, its `--flag`, and the "default checkbox omits the flag" numeric pattern: `DefaultSpinBox` for ints, `DefaultLineEdit` for floats) is owned by the `presets.rs` `Preset` field docs; the per-widget rationale (why top-k is an int SpinBox, the `SegmentedControl`s, the `DefaultLineEdit`'s reveal-on-uncheck) lives in `ui\models_page.slint`.

Field behaviors:

- **Fit to VRAM** (`fit = on|off`, the `-fit` flag) lets llama.cpp size the context and the GPU layers to fit device memory, leaving about 1 GiB free per GPU. The checkbox is live only with **Context** and **GPU layers** both at default, the two things it sizes (`form::fit_allowed`, applied on load and on save too); setting either one by hand switches it off. It is **always written**, `off` unless checked: llama.cpp's own default is on, and a "default" GPU layers here means "offload every layer", which the fit would cut. A preset without the key reads as off. With it on, `tensor-split`, Tensor placement, MoE CPU layers and Dense FFN CPU layers must stay empty too: any of them makes llama.cpp skip the layer placement (`common/fit.cpp`, a warning in the log) and fit only the context.
- **Load mode** (`load-mode`, `-lm`) is **one dropdown, not two checkboxes**: default (the key omitted: llama.cpp's `auto`, mmap unless a device can't), `auto`, `none`, `mmap`, `mlock`, `mmap+mlock`, `dio`. The states do not compose like two bools: `mlock` is mlock *without* mmap (`use_mmap` is true only for `mmap` / `mmap+mlock` / `auto`, `llama-model-loader.cpp`), and two independent toggles cannot express a six-state enum, which is why the UI is a combo.
- **Vision GPU** (`mmproj-device`, `-mmdev`): which GPU the mmproj/CLIP image encoder runs on. A separate dropdown because the encoder is **placed on its own**: it follows the FIRST entry of `--device` (the preset's `device` key included), and with no device pinned llama.cpp gives it the first GPU backend the registry offers, so on a mixed CUDA+ROCm box it lands on the NVIDIA card even when the model is on the AMD one, holds VRAM there for the model's whole lifetime, and computes only on image requests. The key overrides both defaults.
- The **draft device** and **Vision GPU** fields are dropdowns populated from `llama-server --list-devices` (e.g. `CUDA0`, `Vulkan0`). The probe runs async at startup and on Refresh/F5; its result is cached Rust-side (`devices::probed()`), not published as Slint state; the GPU table gets its rows built from it in Rust too (`gpu_split::build_rows`).
- The **GPU layers**, **MoE CPU layers**, **Dense FFN CPU layers**, and **Draft GPU layers** fields are sliders ranging `0..` the model's (or draft's) layer count read from the GGUF header, each with a **default** checkbox (on-screen label; the backing form field is `*_auto`), checked by default, that omits the flag and disables the slider. When the selected draft is an MTP/nextn head (no transformer layers, `block_count 0`) the Draft GPU layers control becomes a two-option on-GPU/on-CPU selector (a `SegmentedControl`, not a `Switch`, so its state is a pure read of the model and never goes stale) instead of a slider.
- Controls that don't apply are disabled: draft n-max, draft p-min, draft n-min, draft n-ubatch (`--spec-draft-n-ubatch`, v0.7.0+: the draft context's physical batch, which reaches embedded MTP heads too) and the two draft K/V cache types need a draft available (a draft model selected, or the main model embedding MTP heads); **draft device and Draft GPU layers need a draft FILE** (see the note below); MoE CPU layers and MoE cache need an MoE model (Dense FFN CPU layers is deliberately not gated the other way: a MoE model still has dense FFN in whichever layers carry no experts, and llama.cpp accepts the flag for both); split-mode needs other than exactly one GPU checked. Spec type is never disabled: the `ngram-*` speculators (`ngram-mod`, `ngram-simple`, `ngram-map-k`, `ngram-map-k4v`) draft from the tokens already in the context, so without a draft they are the only entries it offers; llama.cpp's `ngram-cache` is left out (its draft length is fixed at 8 and the server never saves its lookup caches), and a preset naming it shows `none` while the key round-trips (`presets::spec_type_is_draftless`); their tuning keys sit in the Speculative decoding card below.
- **Every field of the KV cache card can be left at `default`** (i.e. the flag is omitted and llama.cpp decides), but the affordance differs per field, because the shape of "unset" does: cache-ram takes the usual `DefaultSpinBox` checkbox; **K/V cache-type** carry it as the FIRST dropdown entry (`Options.cache_types[0]`, which is *not* the same as picking `f16`: that pins f16 whatever llama.cpp's default becomes); and **flash-attn is a three-pill `default`/`on`/`off` `SegmentedControl`, not a checkbox**, since `--flash-attn` takes `[on|off|auto]` and *defaults to auto* (on where the backend supports it); a bool could only ever reach two of its three states, and the one it couldn't reach was the default. `off` is a real instruction, distinct from unset: it forces the kernel away, which also costs a quantized K/V cache most of its benefit. Leaving these unset used to be unreachable from the GUI, and worse than unreachable: the form filled an absent `cache-type-k` in from the schema default, so a preset that had never named one displayed `q8_0` and *wrote* `q8_0` on the next save of any unrelated field, silently requantizing a cache llama.cpp would have left at f16 (guarded by `form.rs`'s `kv_cache_unset_state_survives_the_form_round_trip`).
- **The K/V cache-type dropdowns offer every type llama.cpp accepts** (`f32 f16 bf16 q8_0 q5_1 q5_0 q4_1 q4_0 iq4_nl`, highest precision first; there is no 6-bit K-quant cache type, q5_1 is the closest), and **a strip on the KV cache card says when the picked pair will not run where the preset runs** (`kv_cache::warning`, Rust-side because it parses the device pins; `AppWindow`'s `changed kv_cache_inputs` handler and every GPU-row rebuild recompute it). Accepted is not runnable, and each gap is silent in llama.cpp: CUDA and ROCm have no FlashAttention kernel for `iq4_nl` (a quantized V forces flash attention on, so the attention goes to the CPU; Vulkan does have one), Vulkan wants `bf16` on K and V together or on neither, a quantized V with flash attention `off` does not load at all, a type llama.cpp does not accept (a hand-edited `q6_K`) fails the load while the combo shows `default`, and on CUDA/ROCm a supported pair with no COMPILED vector kernel converts the whole used cache to f16 on every 1-2 token decode step. That last list is a build setting, `GGML_CUDA_FA_QUANTS` in the repo's `cmake-options.psd1` (the framework adds `q5_0-q5_0` and `q5_1-q5_1` to upstream's `q4_0`/`q8_0`/`f16`/`bf16`), mirrored as `kv_cache::NATIVE_VEC_PAIRS` and pinned to that file by a test. Everything here is a copy of upstream logic, not a probe of the installed DLLs: re-check `ggml-cuda/fattn.cu`, the Vulkan `supports_op` arm and `kv_cache_types` on a llama.cpp bump.
- **A new preset leaves every tunable at `default`**, i.e. the key is omitted and the model runs on llama.cpp's own defaults until you override something on purpose (`Preset::default()`). Pinning one is not inert: `parallel` in particular turns off the unified KV cache llama-server's auto mode enables, quartering the context a single request may use (see the [Integrations tab](#integrations-tab)). The only keys a new preset writes are the four the form has no "unset" affordance for (`jinja`, `mmproj-offload`, `reasoning`, `reasoning-format`), each set to what llama.cpp would have done anyway, so writing them stays a no-op. **Clone** copies the source preset's values, which is its whole point.
- Changes are written to `presets.ini` (or to `presets.ini.disabled` for a preset that is switched off), preserving hand-edits to sections not currently touched. Path fields (model / mmproj / model-draft, and the server side's ModelsDir) containing `;` or `#` are rejected at save: the INI format can't escape them, so they would silently reload truncated (`ini::reject_comment_markers`).

> **Note: embedded MTP heads ignore the draft placement fields.** llama.cpp reads `--device-draft` and `--n-gpu-layers-draft` only when a separate `--model-draft` file is given (both live inside `if (has_dft())`). With MTP heads embedded in the main GGUF (`spec-type` set, no `model-draft`), it builds the draft context against the *target model*, so the draft runs on the model's own GPU and both keys are silently ignored. Setting them there is how a GPU ends up looking assigned to the draft while never drafting a token, so the UI disables them and says so. A **separate** MTP head file (e.g. gemma4-assistant) is the case where they do apply, and there, pin to ONE device: multi-device memory fitting crashes those heads. The full story lives in the `presets.rs` field docs, the single authority for this caveat.

> **Note: the draft context's KV cache does NOT follow the model's, and these two flags are the only way to reach it.** `common_base_params_to_speculative` (`common/speculative.cpp`) builds the draft params by copying the whole struct (the model's `--cache-type-k`/`-v` included) and then *unconditionally overwrites* both with `params.speculative.draft.cache_type_k/_v`, whose own default is `GGML_TYPE_F16` (`common/common.h`). So a preset running the model on a q8_0 KV cache still drafts against an **f16** one, and "leave the draft types unset and they inherit the model" is exactly backwards: unset means f16, always. **Draft K/V cache type** (`--spec-draft-type-k` / `--spec-draft-type-v`, aliases `-ctkd` / `-ctvd`) are the flags that reach it. Note this overwrite sits *outside* the `if (has_draft)` block that guards `devices` / `n_gpu_layers` / `tensor_buft_overrides`, so unlike the placement pair in the note above, these two **do** apply to MTP heads embedded in the main GGUF, which is why they stay enabled there and why `prune_inactive_draft_keys` groups them with the speculator rather than with the placement keys.

### GPU distribution table

`--tensor-split` is a positional vector indexed over the devices named by `--device`, **in `--device` order**. Typing `3,1` into a text box therefore means nothing until you know which devices those are, and with `--device` unset, "those devices" is every detected backend, which on a mixed box is a CUDA card, two ROCm devices (one of them an iGPU) and three duplicate Vulkan views of the same three GPUs. So the two settings share one widget (`GpuSplitTable`, used by both tabs): the **split-mode selector on top** (it decides what the rows below mean), then a row per detected GPU with a checkbox, its VRAM, an editable weight, and the derived share. There is deliberately no flags-preview line under the table: the values are persisted to the INIs, and a preview that showed `--device`/`--tensor-split` while ignoring the mode used to read as the whole truth while being stale.

Rows are built in Rust (`gpu_split::build_rows`): the **checked devices first, in split order**, then the rest in probe order. So the table read top to bottom *is* the `--device` list and its `--tensor-split` vector. Checking a box **appends** the device to the split; the **≡ drag handle** on each checked row is how you reorder it; the weight rides along with its device, so the proportions survive a move.

Position 0 is not cosmetic: it is `devices[0]`, which is also llama.cpp's **`main_gpu`** (it defaults `--main-gpu` to 0 and the framework never overrides it).
